Nothing rendered differently because nothing calls these yet - but a GPU renderer built on the 5.1 contract would have sampled the WRONG TEXTURE on every textured face, and the parse-and-inspect tests would have kept agreeing with it, because the export and the parser agreed with EACH OTHER. **Last updated:** 2026-10-05 (previous - **PHASE 5.2 WAS IMPLEMENTED AND STATIC-GATED; ITS LIVE GATE IS PENDING.** Added **two new files** - `ui/GpuFloatBuffer.java` and `ui/GpuIntBuffer.java` - with **zero call sites and no existing source file touched**: growable primitive buffers with `put` overloads, a bulk `arraycopy` path, `ensureCapacity`, and `clear`. ⚠ **The design point that matters: they exist so a steady frame REUSES its backing array, and that is pinned by ARRAY IDENTITY rather than by capacity - a capacity that merely happened to match would still hide a reallocation.** `clear()` resets the position and keeps the storage, because **emptying must not mean freeing**. ⚠ **Two classes rather than one generic buffer because Java arrays are not covariant over primitives** - a single type would either box every value (the allocation this path exists to avoid) or store `Object[]` and cast on every access. ⚠⚠ **The cost of that is divergence risk, and it is answered rather than ignored: the harness runs an IDENTICAL write sequence through both buffers and asserts identical positions AND capacities.** ⚠ **Contract mirrors 5.1: `array()` is LIVE storage, `get()` FAILS FAST past the position** (a buffer returning stale data is the classic silent upload corruption), **and there is deliberately no limit/flip - a write always appends past `position()`.** **Static gates: harness 94 -> 118/118** (24 new), `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly **100**. ⚠ **Mutation-proved four ways, each failing exactly its own assertion - including the one that matters most: changing ONLY `GpuIntBuffer`'s growth policy to `+1` is caught by the symmetry test, so the divergence guard is NOT vacuous.** ⚠ **Still no GL code, as 5.2 requires - the upload representation (direct vs heap) is a Phase 7 decision, deliberately not pre-empted. and hooked it into `Model.method443` as an early dispatch. ⚠️ **The design decision, and the reason this is a 5-line change rather than a 22-site refactor: `method443` IS the per-model submission point** (one call means "draw this model, placed here"; it loops the model's own triangles internally), so the hook goes at its top and **every caller is covered without a single call site changing**. ⚠️ **I prefer this to routing the 21 call sites in `method314` precisely because a missed call site would silently keep a software-only path, whereas an early dispatch cannot be bypassed.** It also mirrors the pattern 4.1a already set for the present (`Renderer.presentGameFrame` asks `GlPresent` first, then falls through to the software blit). ⚠️ **Proof the step is behaviour-neutral, measured rather than argued: the source diff is 5 insertions and 0 deletions in `Model.java`** (1 import + 3 dispatch lines + 2 comment lines), **`Texture.java`, `DrawingArea.java`, `Framebuffer.java` and `WorldController.java` are byte-clean, and BOTH pinned hashes are unmoved** - the 4.1b framebuffer hash and the 4.1c-2a triangle hash. ⚠️ **The harness assertions were written to pin a property rather than to accompany the code: one proves dispatch DECLINES with nothing installed (so the seam is inert), and one proves an installed stub DOES intercept `method443`.** ⚠️ **That second assertion is stronger than it looks and I chose the fixture for exactly that reason: `Model.aModel_1621` is an empty model that `method443` would otherwise CULL and return from before drawing anything, so the stub can only be reached if the dispatch sits at the very top - the test pins WHERE the hook is, not merely that it exists.** **Live gate:** `Record.bat` vs the unmodified server -> capture verified real and distinct (**3,848 units / 3,826 R / 22 sent**) -> **VERDICT: COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 99.0% - the highest of the whole refactor - exit 0** -> user confirmed **every model rendered exactly as before** (objects, walls, roofs, trees, NPCs, players). **Static gates:** `clean check installBin` green, harness **52 -> 56/56**, warnings exactly the **100** baseline. ⚠️ **Not claimed: the GROUND is not seamed.** the ground is drawn by TWO methods that never go through `method443`: `WorldController.method315` (mesh `Class43`, 7 rasteriser calls) and `WorldController.method316` (mesh `Class40`, 4), so this hook covers models only - objects, walls, roofs, NPCs and players, but not the floor the player walks on.

**Last thing I did:** 2026-10-05 - **EXECUTED 4.1c-2a: EXTENDED THE GATE TO COVER THE TRIANGLE RASTERISER, AND MUTATION-TESTED IT AGAINST THE REAL CODE.** The gap being closed is precise: 4.1b pins the generic 2D substrate (`DrawingArea` + `Sprite`), which says nothing about the 3D triangle path, so `Model.method443` and everything under it were gated by the live screen alone. ⚠️⚠️ **The enabling discovery, and it is what makes this cheap: `Model.method443` delegates to exactly THREE public static entry points - `Texture.method374` (flat, 4 call sites), `method376` (4) and `method378` (8) - and they take plain integers.** So the rasteriser can be driven directly with fixed coordinates and **no `Model` object is needed at all**, which matters because `Model` is not headlessly constructible: all six constructors are private, and the only byte-array route (`method460` -> `method462`) would require hand-synthesising the model binary format - intricate and fragile, so I deliberately did not go that way. ⚠️ **Three facts that only running it could establish, each of which would otherwise have produced a broken or dishonest gate: (1) the colours are 16-BIT model face colours indexing a 65536-entry palette, NOT 24-bit RGB** - my first attempt passed `0xFF0000` and threw `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 65643 out of bounds for length 65536`, because the index is derived as `j1 >> 8` from a value already scaled past the table; **(2) `Texture.method364()` is the required setup** - it derives the row-offset table and texture centres from the CURRENT `DrawingArea` size, so it must run after `initDrawingArea`; and **(3) ⚠️⚠️ `Texture.method372` is the WRONG way to build the palette, because its first statement is `d += Math.random() * 0.03 - 0.015`.** That last one is the subtle one and it failed the determinism test on the first run: **the client's real texture palette is deliberately jittered on every load**, so any gate built on it would be non-reproducible while still looking green in a single run. The harness now fills `anIntArray1482` deterministically itself, which is the correct call because this gate pins the RASTERISER - geometry, span stepping, the write path - not the palette. ⚠️ **One more deliberate choice: `Fog.sceneDepth` is set to 0 so `method374`'s guard (`Fog.sceneDepth > 50 && client.fogStrength > 0`) short-circuits on its first operand, which keeps the gate from loading the `client` class at all** - `client` carries heavy static initialisers and is not safely loadable headlessly, so this is a correctness constraint rather than a micro-optimisation. **Verified:** the workload is deterministic across two separate JVM processes (hash `57838498b8af2b9c834b7a82bcd37305712f1014665926449b3aaa9c88732d67` both times), the gate has teeth on its own input (one altered triangle colour changes the hash), and ⚠️ **the mutation test was run against the REAL rasteriser rather than my own workload** - a one-bit change at `Texture.method375`'s pixel write (`ai[i] = j;` -> `ai[i] = j ^ 1;`) failed the pinned assertion with `NOTE Triangle rasterisation changed`, so the gate is not merely self-consistent. Harness **49 -> 52/52**, `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly the **100** baseline, and `Texture.java` was restored byte-clean afterwards (the revert needed a `git checkout` because my script's rewrite normalised CRLF to LF - content-identical, but the tree is now verified clean). ⚠️ **Stated gap rather than implied coverage: the gate drives the FLAT path (`method374`).** The textured paths (`method376`, `method378`) need the texture pixel tables from `Texture.method371`/`method317` and are NOT yet covered.

**Last thing I did:** 2026-10-05 - **EXECUTED 4.1c-1 (THE RASTERISATION SEAM, SUBSTRATE ONLY) AND LIVE-VERIFIED IT.** Extracted the 2D substrate out of `DrawingArea` into a new `ui/Framebuffer.java` (**417 lines**: 10 state fields - `pixels`, `width`, `height`, `topX`, `topY`, `bottomX`, `bottomY`, `centerX`, `centerY`, `anInt1387` - plus **all 18 primitives**, including the alpha-blend paths `method335`/`method340`/`method342` and the public helpers `drawLine`/`blendHLine`), leaving `DrawingArea` as a **27-line subclass** (`DrawingArea extends Framebuffer extends NodeSub`). ⚠️ **The deliberate design choice, and the reason this landed clean: an EXTRACT-SUPERCLASS rather than an extract-and-repoint.** Static members are inherited, so every existing reference keeps resolving - `DrawingArea.width`, `DrawingArea.pixels`, and the unqualified inherited uses inside `Sprite`/`Texture`/`TextDrawingArea`/`Background` - which means **the ~570 field reads across 17 files did not need touching at all.** ⚠️ **I verified that two ways rather than trusting the compile: (1) the compiler was clean on the FIRST try, which is what a true no-call-site move predicts; (2) a grep audit shows `git status` changed ONLY `DrawingArea.java` + added `Framebuffer.java`, and ZERO files reference `Framebuffer.` directly.** ⚠️⚠️ **The 4.1b gate is what made this safe to do at all: the framebuffer hash came back UNMOVED (`775ffc78...a9e39e`) after 395 lines and 10 mutable statics moved - byte-identical output.** Without the gate, the only evidence for a change this broad would have been "it looked fine", which is the exact trap that has already burned this repo once. **Live gate:** `Record.bat` vs the unmodified server -> capture verified real and distinct (**4,434 units / 4,389 R / 45 sent**) -> **VERDICT: COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600 (the strongest alignment of the whole refactor), LCS 96.7%, exit 0** -> user confirmed it rendered **exactly as before, nothing different at all**, which is the correct result for a move designed to be invisible. **Static gates:** compile clean first try, `clean check installBin` green, harness **49/49**, warnings exactly the **100** baseline, cfg in sync. ⚠️ **Not claimed: this does NOT make the renderer swappable.** `Framebuffer` still holds `static` state and there is still one global buffer; a GPU path would need the storage to be owned by an instance, which is a separate, larger change (4.1c-2).

(3) `getDataForName` decompresses a declared range **with no bound against the buffer** (`:91-99`), reachable when the index disagrees with the data length, and the LZ branch at `:58` is likewise outside any `try`. ⚠️ **`Stream`'s silent fallbacks are confirmed and deliberately still untouched** (`readUnsignedWord()`'s `catch` → hardcoded **1795**, `readUnsignedByte2()` → **-263**, `method421()` → **-1**, and `writeBytes`' catch rethrowing a bare `RuntimeException` and discarding the cause) — because every reader and every packet parse goes through them, replacing the sentinel is its own deliberately-tested change. ⚠️⚠️ **FLAGGED CONTRADICTION (needs a decision before 2.3 is implemented): this phase's own text — "replace silent hardcoded magic return values with safe bounded handling" — collides with the frozen-contract rule, which says a refactor may MOVE wire/cache code but "must never change what it reads or in what order".** `readUnsignedWord()`/`readUnsignedByte2()`/`method421()` are the **sole** accessors for every packet field and every cache definition, so their exact out-of-range return values *are* the observable behaviour on a desync or a truncated archive. ⚠️ **The distinction that resolves it, so the next session does not have to re-derive it: the contract constrains *valid* input only — how the client reacts to corrupt data is undefined and therefore fair game — but a sentinel is also reachable on *valid-looking* input mid-desync, so changing it changes how a desync presents rather than merely hardening a corrupt path.** That is why the **`StreamLoader` bound fixes are safe to do now (they only reject inputs the contract never defined) while the `Stream` sentinel replacement is NOT a defensive tweak** and should either be dropped from 2.3 or split out as its own explicitly-approved behaviour change. ⚠️ **No code changed this step**, so nothing needs re-verifying; the counts below are unchanged. **Before that,** ✅ **Phase 2.2 (the consumer side) — the five `ObjectManager` sites are FIXED, failing-test-first and mutation-pinned, and the session's finding is that they were never a crash at all.** ⚠️ **This plan previously claimed these "throw during region load". That was WRONG, and the correction is the whole insight:** `method171`'s entire body (36-450) is wrapped in `try { ... } catch(Exception e) { }` **with an empty handler**, so an out-of-range floor id surfaces nowhere — it quietly abandons the rest of the region's blending and lighting and writes nothing: **no crash, no message, no log**. ⚠️ **That inverted the test design.** "Does not throw" is vacuous when the method swallows its own exceptions, which is exactly the trap the first `ObjectDef` test fell into, so the test had to assert on a **later side effect of the same pass** instead. ⚠️ **The fix is one helper — `boundedFloorId(int)` — called where each id is read**, and the off-by-one at `:242` is the key to reading it: the id is **1-based** (0 means "no floor"), so the correct comparison is `id > Flo.cache.length`, while the existing guard tested the already-decremented `i19 - 1 > Flo.cache.length` and therefore let `Flo.cache[length]` through. The witness takes the untextured branch and the underlay tile resolves to `Flo.cache.length`, so `:244` skips the texture/render block for `i19 - 1 == 54` — **no textures needed.** ⚠️ **Failing-first and fully mutation-tested:** before the fix the test FAILED with `witness shading 0x0`; after it, `0x924`. Harness **20 → 24/24**, warnings still **100**. ⚠️ **Also found and deliberately deferred to 2.2, not silently fixed:** an id inside the array's range but never defined by the cache reads `streamIndices[i] == 0` and parses garbage from `obj.dat` offset 0 — not the reported crash, and treating it as invalid is a behaviour change needing verification against the real cache. **Before that,** ✅ **Phase 0.3 + 0.5 — the golden master exists, is validated, and now has a re-runnable check.** The capture is **9,470 protocol units (365 sent / 9,105 received, largest 171 bytes)**, stored at `Proxy Client/golden-master/session-20261004.log` and **validated by decoding its first 12 units against `client.java:10409-10461`**: a 2-byte `0e00`, then exactly **9** single-byte reads (the code does 8 discarded `read()`s plus 1 response code — the log matches, so the tap is proven to sit in the right place rather than assumed to), then the 8-byte seed flush, then the **171-byte** login block beginning `10 a9 ff 013d` — opcode 16, length 0xa9, `0xff`, and **317**. ⚠️⚠️ **The correction, and it matters: I had claimed the log was diffable, but a byte-for-byte comparison is IMPOSSIBLE on this protocol** — every opcode after login is ISAAC-encrypted with keys seeded from `Math.random()`, and the login block embeds those seeds, so two sessions share neither opcodes nor handshake bytes. **0.5 therefore compares STRUCTURE (direction + length), which is exactly what a desync changes.** `tools/compare-capture.ps1` + `gradlew compareCapture` were **mutation-tested, not assumed: self-compare = 0 divergences/exit 0; 3 units deleted = 3,093 divergences/first at unit 4737/exit 1; a single unit resized `R 6`→`R 7` = 1 divergence/exit 1** — the last is the one that matters, because it proves sensitivity to a length change alone rather than only to gross truncation. Also verified **CRLF-proof**, since git converts the golden master on checkout (a CRLF copy still compares identical). ⚠️ **Two bugs found and fixed on the way:** the capture's own filename was `packet-tap-.log` because `%STAMP%` inside a parenthesised block expands at *parse* time (fixed with a `goto` branch — now resolves `packet-tap-20261004-153650.log`), and `PacketTap`'s javadoc carried the same wrong "diffable" claim, now corrected. ⚠️ **The capture is safe to commit:** the username and password are RSA-encrypted in place by `stream.doKeys()` before sending, so the file holds ciphertext. **Before that,** ✅ **Built the golden-master capture tooling (0.3) so the last Phase 0 item is a short job rather than a research task** — `PacketTap.java` + `Record.bat`, plus 6 new harness assertions (**20/20**). ⚠️ **The design decision that matters:** I tapped `RSSocket`'s `read()`/`flushInputStream()`/`queueBytes()` rather than the raw socket, because **TCP chunk boundaries are not reproducible** — the same session splits its writes differently on every run, so a chunk-level log cannot be diffed and would have been a useless oracle. The client consumes the stream one protocol unit at a time, so tapping there captures the protocol *as the client understands it*, which is reproducible. ⚠️ **I verified rather than assumed that normal play is unaffected:** with `-Dsoultrail.packettap` unset, `enabled()` returns false and no file is created. ⚠️ **And I self-tested the tap**, including that every data line matches `<dir> <len> <hex>` with **no timestamps** — a log with a clock or a counter in it cannot be diffed, and a tap that silently wrote nothing would have cost the one chance to record that session. Also confirmed the class-load smoke test at **214/214** with **0 baseline classes lost**. ⚠️ **Recorded the baseline: the user confirms Nex and the 667 content work correctly right now**, which is what every later phase must preserve. ✅ **Phase 1 verified live** — the user ran the Gradle-built client against the server and reported "all works fine," so 1.1–1.4 are ticked on evidence. ⚠️ **Then the user revealed something that corrects this plan's core assumption, and investigating it took the rest of the session.** The client is **not** a pure 474 client: it is a **474 base carrying 667 (and 647) content injected through four separate ad-hoc mechanisms**, and the stated plan is to add more while keeping 474. I traced all four before writing anything down rather than taking the description at face value: **(a)** 667 NPCs — `Nex` at ids **13447+**, `Max` at 650, `Callisto`, `Blood reaver` — are **hardcoded switch cases in `EntityDef`** (471 lines, 10 cases, 15 uses of the **90xxx** custom-model range, e.g. `252 → 90252` "so Raw preload cannot clobber OG model 252"); **(b)** `CurseData667` injects 667 curse seq/spotanim/models from a `CursePack/` cache folder, **remapping frame ids into `Class36` slots 28000+** so they cannot overwrite whip/godsword/run frames sharing the same 667 file ids; **(c)** `Class36.loadFrames()` additionally **auto-loads any loose numeric `{id}.dat` in the cache root with id ≥ 100** via a **647-named reader** (`load_647`), e.g. `3502.dat` for Nex; **(d)** `SpotAnim.readValues667` coexists with the 474 reader, and `CurseData667` **hand-reimplements 667 opcode skipping**. ⚠️ **Why this mattered enough to change the plan rather than just be noted:** the contract table listed "the cache format" as one frozen surface, but there are four sources relying on three undocumented collision-avoidance schemes, so a refactor that reshuffles `Class36` slots, model load order, or the `EntityDef` id range can **silently corrupt 474 content with no crash and no error** — the worst failure mode this plan exists to prevent. I added a contract subsection, a Phase 6.5 for the user's 667 direction, and corrected Phase 6.1 (whose scope was understated). ⚠️ **I also answered the user's question about the "634 characters" option by checking it rather than guessing: the whole chooser is inert.** The label says 634, the code passes `"667"`, and `client.main` forwards args to `new Jframe(args)` — **whose constructor never reads them**. Both buttons launch a byte-identical client. **Before that,** **Phase 1 — the client has a real Gradle build, and building it uncovered four player-visible strings that were being compiled as mojibake.** ⚠️ **1.1/1.2:** `Proxy Client/build.gradle` + `settings.gradle` + the server's Gradle **9.8.0** wrapper (already cached in `~/.gradle`, so this is offline). `-source 1.7` is gone, replaced by `options.release = 8`; the class-file version moved **51 → 52** on all 206 freshly built classes, `installBin` populates `bin/`, and **`Run.bat` is untouched**. `Compile.bat` is kept as a working fallback with a deprecation header rather than deleted. ⚠️ **A deliberate deviation from this plan:** no `toolchain` block. The client's correct target is Java 8 and `javac 25 --release 8` already emits it, so a toolchain would add a moving part without changing output — and the target is now the one-line override `-PclientJavaRelease=17`. (Correcting my own first note: a toolchain *was* available, since Gradle already caches a JDK 21 from the server build; it is simply unnecessary.) Before relying on `--release` I checked the hazard it *introduces* — it narrows the **API** to Java 8's documented surface, which breaks any use of internal `sun.*` APIs — and confirmed the client imports none. ⚠️ **The find:** 13 of the 131 sources contain non-ASCII, and in 4 of them it was a UTF-8 em dash inside a **string literal**. Read as Cp1252 (the platform default, because `Compile.bat` sets no `-encoding`), each became three wrong characters, so the client renders `"â€” click an item"`, `"Friends â€” 3/200"`, and two tooltips. Compiling both ways proved **exactly those 4 classes change and no others**, and the fix is verified present in the rebuilt `bin/`. ⚠️ **1.3:** warning baseline recorded at **0 errors / 100 warnings** (77 serial, 9 rawtypes, 5 unchecked, 5 deprecation, 2 static, 1 empty), reproduced independently with a direct `javac` run using identical flags. ⚠️ **1.4:** `ObjectCollisionSizes` now embeds the SHA-256 of the `objectSize.cfg` it came from, and `gradlew check` fails if it ever drifts — mutation-tested (all-zero hash fails loudly naming both hashes, restore passes). Regenerating changed **exactly 8 lines and left the 28 KB collision table byte-identical**. ⚠️ **Whole build verified:** `gradlew clean build` green, harness **14/14**, **213/213 classes load**, and **no baseline class was lost**. ⚠️ **Phase 1 is deliberately NOT ticked:** the client rule requires a live client against the server, and `bin/` has changed. **Before that,** **Phase 0.1, 0.2 and 0.4 — the client now has a verified safety net, and for the first time, tests.** ⚠️ **0.1:** backed up `Proxy Client/` to `_client-backup-20261004-145741`, checked **byte-exact** — **423 files *and* 6,415,094 bytes matching the original**, not merely "the copy command returned success". ⚠️ **0.2:** wrote `BIN-BASELINE.sha256` (**213** classes) and `SRC-BASELINE.sha256` (**131** sources) into that backup. ⚠️ **0.4, the one that mattered:** built `Proxy Client/test/ClientHarness.java` and `Proxy Client/RunTests.bat`, which run **14/14 green headlessly against the compiled client in `bin/`** — no display, no server, no game loop — covering `Stream` round-trips, **byte order** (asserted against the raw buffer, because a round-trip passes even if both halves are little-endian), sign-extension, 3-byte reads, the `g2`/`g4` aliases and bit access. ⚠️ **I mutation-tested the harness**, because "the tests pass" is worth nothing if the harness cannot fail: corrupting a single expectation gave `passed: 13 failed: 1`, `RESULT: FAIL` and **exit code 1**. **Two findings fell out of this** and are now recorded under "Known broken": client sources must stay **ASCII** (`javac` here defaults to Cp1252 and rejected my harness for containing a warning symbol and em dashes), and `Stream.readUnsignedWord()` **silently returns a hardcoded 1795** on an out-of-range read rather than throwing — now pinned as a Phase 2 tripwire. **Before that,** **Phase 0.6 — committed the seven untracked load-bearing client classes** (`f3ec2e1`). Before committing I re-verified the claim rather than trusting my own earlier note, because the consequence of being wrong was a large commit against a tree with no fallback: `client.java` really is unmodified vs `HEAD`, it really does reference `LwjglPresent` and `OverlayRefresh`, all seven files really do exist on disk, and **none were gitignored** (an ignored file would have made this a `-f` problem rather than an untracked one). ⚠️ **My first enumeration was wrong and I caught it:** a `git status --porcelain | Where-Object { $_ -match '^\?\?' }` filter returned *nothing*, which would have read as "already tracked". `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` is the authoritative form and returned all seven — the lesson is that a filter on porcelain text is a convenience, and `ls-files` is the check. The commit is **add-only: 1,907 insertions, 0 deletions, 0 modifications**, so the 46 files of pre-existing uncommitted client work are untouched. After it, all seven are in `HEAD`, no untracked `.java` remains under `Proxy Client/src`, and the client's `HEAD` can finally compile. ⚠️ **While verifying I also checked the server and it was never affected** — every file referencing its untracked `ConnectionPool`/`MusicState` is itself modified, so this was a client-only fault; worth recording, because the obvious assumption was that both trees were equally broken. ⚠️ **Separately, `git status` reports 1,538 pending deletions repo-wide; I checked they are pre-existing and entirely outside the client** (`mystic-updatedclient/`, `Website/forum hooks`, `Pictures/Crowns`, `POS_BACKUP/`, plus the two server `ShutdownHook` files Phase 5 deleted deliberately). My commit deleted nothing.

Before that, **wrote this plan.** Before writing it I measured the client rather than trusting the brief, and **two findings changed the shape of the work**, so they are stated up front:

1. ⚠️ **The client already runs on Java 25 — only the *compile target* is ancient.** `Run.bat` launches a bare `java`, and the default JDK on this machine is **25.0.4**, so the game is already running on a modern JVM (and it works — it was played during the server session). The only thing stuck in the past is `Compile.bat`'s `-source 1.7 -target 1.7`, and that still works **only because `Compile.bat` hardcodes JDK 8's `javac`** (`C:\Program Files\Java\jdk1.8.0_202\bin\javac.exe`). Plain `javac` on PATH is 25, which **rejects `-source 1.7` outright** (support for source/target 7 was removed in JDK 21). So the build works by accident of two different JVMs being involved, and the Java upgrade is a *build* problem, not a *runtime survival* problem.
2. ⚠️ **Java version has literally nothing to do with server compatibility.** The user's stated top priority is "no issues with compatibility with the server". The client and server are **separate JVMs talking over a socket**, so their Java versions need not match and cannot conflict. The real contract is the **wire protocol and the cache format** — see below. This matters because it means the riskiest-sounding item (the Java upgrade) is *not* where the server risk lives, and the item that *is* risky (anything touching packet or cache reading) is small and testable.
3. **`openGlEnabled` is not OpenGL.** The client has no 3D GPU path at all. `openGlEnabled`/`GlPresent` only switch on Java2D's `sun.java2d.opengl` pipeline — i.e. accelerated **blitting of a software-rasterised framebuffer**. The `LWJGL` jar in `deps/` is **LWJGL 2.x and is imported by zero source files**.
4. ⚠️ **LWJGL + AWT parenting was already tried here and deliberately abandoned.** `LwjglPresent.java` is a stub whose every method returns `false`, with this comment: *"LWJGL `Display.setParent` is not used. Parenting a native GL window onto the AWT applet deadlocks Windows when the frame loses or gains focus (both AWT and LWJGL pump the same HWND)."* **Any OpenGL plan that begins with `Display.setParent` is re-walking a known dead end.** Phase 7 is built around that lesson.
5. **One of the two reference repos the user supplied is effectively empty.** `ubjelly/317-OpenGL` contains three files (~2.7 KB total) and no renderer. `Elvarg-Client-Public` is a full RuneLite client and is the only usable reference. Details in the review section.
6. ⚠️⚠️ **The client's committed state (`HEAD`) cannot compile — the working tree is load-bearing.** This is the most urgent thing in this document. `client.java` is **unmodified vs `HEAD`** yet references **`LwjglPresent` and `OverlayRefresh`**, and **neither is tracked by git at all**. In total the client has **seven untracked classes that tracked source depends on**, and they are heavily used: `OverlayRefresh` is referenced by **39 files**, `GroundItemLists` by **20**, `ChatboxItemSearch` by **18**, `HiscoresPanel` by **6**, `CurseData667` by **3**, `LwjglPresent` (by `client.java`) and `JavaUncompress` by **1** each. **A fresh clone, `git clean -fd`, or `git checkout .` therefore destroys the client build outright.** This is the same "untracked-until-lost" pattern the server plan already recorded as having destroyed two cluster tools — it has now hit the client, and here it is not a tool but the product. `Phase 0.6` commits them. ⚠️ **The server is *not* affected:** `ConnectionPool` (untracked `server/util/`) and `MusicState` (untracked) are referenced **only from files that are themselves modified in the working tree**, so the server's `HEAD` still compiles. The asymmetry is worth remembering before assuming the two trees are in the same state.

**Verification:** ✅ **As of 2026-10-04 the client has a real build, headless tests, and a protocol oracle.** `gradlew clean build` is green: harness **46/46** (`gradlew harness`), stale-class report (`gradlew reportStaleClasses`), and cfg-freshness (`gradlew verifyObjectCollisionSizes`) all run as part of `gradlew check`. ⚠️ **Harness coverage moved from one class to ten (`Stream`, `PacketTap`, `ItemDef`, `EntityDef`, `ObjectDef`, `VarBit`, `Varp`, `IDK`, `ObjectManager`, `StreamLoader`) — but "ten of 131 sources" is still the honest framing**, and `client.java`, `Model` and the cache readers remain uncovered. ⚠️ **And the protocol oracle now refuses to be vacuous: `compare-capture.ps1` fails (exit 2) when the two captures hash the same, because that means it was handed the golden master twice and its "IDENTICAL" would have meant nothing** — which is exactly what happened on 2026-10-04 when no fresh capture existed, and is why a re-record is now a required step rather than a formality. ⚠️ **The synthetic-cache tests are the strongest ones so far, and the reason is worth keeping:** they build a **valid synthetic cache** instead of leaving the cache unloaded, because the unloaded path short-circuits before the check being tested — so they would have passed with the bug still present. ⚠️ **2.2 then added the sharper lesson: passing tests can still be vacuous.** My first `ObjectDef` test asserted the right property against the wrong input (`forID(-1)`) — because `ObjectDef`'s constructor defaults `type` to `-1`, the cache scan at the top of `forID` returns *before* the clamp — and it **passed with the clamp deleted**. Mutation testing is the only reason that was caught; the rewritten test (`forID(-2)`, then asserting it is the *same object* as `forID(0)`) is itself mutation-proven to fail with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` and exit 1. ⚠️ **The harness tests `build/classes/java/main`, NOT `bin/`** — and `bin/` is what `Run.bat` plays. The two differ until `installBin` runs, which would mean *testing one thing and playing another*. That is not hypothetical: after the 2.1 edit `bin/ItemDef.class` was **stale**, so a live session at that moment would have exercised the old crashing code and produced a misleading result. Caught by disassembling `bin/` and `build/` and comparing; `installBin` was run and the two guard bodies are now byte-identical. **Run `installBin` (or a full `build`) before any live test.** ⚠️ **The 0.3 capture tooling is itself tested** (6 of those 20 assertions), including a check that its log has **no timestamps** (a clock or counter would defeat even the structural comparison, since every line would then differ) — and that `PacketTap` is inert when disabled. ⚠️ **Every one of those checks was mutation-tested** — the harness fails with exit code 1 on a wrong expectation, and the cfg check fails loudly on a tampered hash — because a check that has never been seen to fail is not evidence. Additional one-off verification: all **213/213 `bin/` classes load** (catches missing supertypes a green compile hides), and **no baseline class was lost** against the Phase 0.2 manifest. ⚠️ **But the harness still covers one class (`Stream`) out of 131 sources** — it proves the method, not the coverage. ✅ **`gradlew compareCapture` now DOES establish protocol compatibility — structurally.** It diffs a fresh capture against `golden-master/session-20261004.log` and is mutation-tested to fail on a deleted unit or on **a single resized unit**. ⚠️ **But it cannot see payload contents** (the same ISAAC/`Math.random()` encryption reason), so it cannot detect a wrong animation: ⚠️ **474 asset integrity is still NOT automatically protected**, and the screen-recording half of 0.3 is the only thing that covers it. ⚠️ **It also only means anything for the same route and roughly the same world state** — server tick timing and NPC positions legitimately shift units, so a dirty run is a signal to look, not automatic proof of a bug. ✅ **As of 2026-10-04 evening that "signal to look" is an actual verdict:** the tool scores **common-prefix length + LCS similarity** rather than raw positional divergence, because a fresh human session can never match the golden master positionally — one extra client packet shifts every unit after it. Calibrated on the real captures (89.1% same-protocol/different-route vs a 60-62% destroyed-structure floor; threshold 75%, plus a bounded 600-unit prefix ALIGNMENT — the earlier positional prefix gate was proven false on same-binary captures and replaced, see 0.5). ⚠️ **Stated limit: a small change confined to the route-dependent tail is indistinguishable from a different route and will pass; a break in the login/load prefix or any cascade (which is what a real desync is) will not.**

(The server's `HEAD` was unaffected throughout — verified.)
- ⚠️ **A malformed noted-item definition hard-crashed the client** — `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index -1 ... ItemDef.forID`. Reported live on 2026-10-04 while ranging Rock Crabs. `ItemDef.readValues` takes `certTemplateID` from opcode **98** and `certID` from opcode **97** *separately* and leaves both at `-1`; an entry with 98 but no 97 made `forID` call `toNote()`, which called `forID(certID)` = `forID(-1)`, and line 178 did `streamIndices[-1]`. **This was pre-existing and unrelated to the server refactor** (the server cannot send it: every ground-item send passes `ItemHandler.createGroundItem`'s `if (itemId > 0)` guard, and `ItemAssistant.dropAllItems`' `-1` is filtered there). 🟡 **GUARDED AND LIVE-VERIFIED (Phase 2.1, 2026-10-04)** — implemented, mutation-tested, and confirmed on a live session (logged in, played, exited code 0). `forID` now returns a shared defaulted stand-in for any out-of-cache id, so **every** caller is protected rather than just `toNote`, and `toNote` additionally bails out on a missing cert id or null/empty name. ⚠️ **Still worth knowing that nothing prevents the malformed definition itself** — the guard makes it harmless rather than impossible; the underlying `obj.dat` entry is unchanged.
- ⚠️ **The same shape was real elsewhere too — four instances found, fixed and pinned in Phase 2.2; some remain unaudited.** The pattern is any code that indexes an array or a `Stream` with a cache-derived value. ✅ **Fixed (2.2), all three crashing during cache load at startup:** **`VarBit.unpackConfig`** indexed `Varp.cache` with the target varp id read from `varbit.dat`; **`Varp.readValues`** wrote into its index table past the end when one definition carried opcode 3 twice; **`IDK.readValues`** wrote `anIntArray659/660/661[i - 40/50/60]` through opcode ranges ten slots wide into six/six/five-slot arrays, so opcodes 46-49, 56-59 and 65-69 crashed. ⚠️ **The `IDK` fix also had to preserve the read while skipping the store** — folding the read into the guard would desync `idk.dat` silently, and a dedicated test now pins that. ⚠️ **Also fixed (2.2): `ObjectManager`'s five `Flo.cache[id - 1]` sites** (`:113`, `:127`, `:189`, `:222`, `:243`), where `id` is an overlay byte from the landscape data and only `id > 0` was checked. ⚠️ **These did NOT crash — they failed SILENTLY**, because `method171` wraps its whole body (36-450) in a `try` whose `catch` is **empty**, so an out-of-range id abandoned the rest of that region's blending and lighting with no message, no log and no crash. **The plan's earlier "throws during region load" claim was wrong** and is corrected in the 2.2 section; the guard is now a single `boundedFloorId` clamp applied where each id is read. ⚠️ **Still outstanding:** the `streamIndices[i] == 0` case (an id in range that the cache never defined parses from `obj.dat` offset 0); ⚠️ `ObjectDef.forID(-1)` returns an uninitialized cache entry for the same reason (`type` defaults to `-1`); ⚠️ `CurseData667` indexes `Animation.anims[]` with an anim id derived from `spotanim.dat` (667 path — Phase 6.5). ⚠️ **`EntityDef`, `ObjectDef`, `Flo`, `Animation`, `SpotAnim`, `Class36.method531` and `client.java`'s `safeAnimation`/`safeGfxAnimation` were checked and are already guarded.**

Editing the cfg without regenerating silently drifts client collision from server collision. The generator validates that no footprint exceeds 4 bits per axis.

It matters only because it must not be mistaken for a real revision switch when reasoning about the data model — 667 content is always loaded.
- ⚠️ **The 667/647 injection layers rely on undocumented ID-avoidance conventions** with nothing enforcing them — `Class36` slots **28000+** for CursePack frames, **90xxx** for remapped models, NPC ids **13447+**. Two silent-failure modes follow: a new injection colliding with 474 data, or Phase 8 deleting a "magic number" that is actually load-bearing. Added to the frozen-contract section above; Phase 6.5 is where this gets designed properly.
- ✅ ~~⚠️ **Client source files must stay ASCII.**~~ **This was WRONG and is corrected here.** Thirteen of the 131 sources have contained non-ASCII all along. The accurate finding is: **the build's source encoding is load-bearing, and it was silently wrong.** `Compile.bat` passes no `-encoding`, so `javac` uses the platform default (**Cp1252 here**) while the sources are **UTF-8**. In nine files those bytes sit only in comments, so bytecode was unaffected — which is why this went unnoticed. **Phase 1.2 fixes the build to read UTF-8**, which also matches `tools/generate-object-collision-sizes.ps1`, which already *writes* UTF-8. ⚠️ **The lesson is worth keeping:** I first read the symptom ("my file won't compile because of a warning symbol") as a rule to obey rather than a signal to investigate, and the real bug was in the shipped client, not in my file.
- ✅ ~~⚠️ **Four player-visible strings are compiled as mojibake.**~~ **FOUND AND FIXED (Phase 1.2, commit pending).** Reading UTF-8 bytes as Cp1252 turned each em dash (`E2 80 94`) into three wrong characters, and four of them were in **string literals**, so players saw the corruption:
  - `ChatboxItemSearch` — `"<mojibake> click an item"` → `"— click an item"`
  - `FriendListPlugin` — `"Friends <mojibake> 3/200"` → `"Friends — 3/200"`
  - `Interfaces` — `"Crisp edge smoothing only <mojibake> no screen blur"`
  - `PluginSidebar` — `"Crisp silhouette edges only <mojibake> no blur"`
  ⚠️ **The blast radius was measured before the fix, not assumed:** compiling the same sources both ways changed **exactly those 4 classes and no others**, and the new `bin/` was re-checked to confirm a real U+2014 is now present and the mojibake is gone. ⚠️ **This is a genuine behaviour change** — the first thing in this plan that alters what a player sees — which is exactly why Phase 1.1 is not marked done without a live session.
- ⚠️ **`bin/` carries 7 stale `.class` files that no source produces**: `Data667.class` (an orphan with **zero references anywhere** — `CurseData667` is a different, live class), `LwjglPresent$1..$5.class` (anonymous inner classes from before `LwjglPresent` was reduced to a stub, so they are unreachable), and `PluginSidebar$PluginRow$4.class` (same, for a `PluginRow` that now has fewer anonymous classes). Cause: `Compile.bat`'s `robocopy /E /IS /IT` **never purges**, exactly as the Phase 0.2 note predicted. ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT deleted by the new build**: purging changes what ships, and `LwjglPresent$1..$5` also corroborate that the abandoned LWJGL attempt was once real code. The Gradle build reports them (`gradlew reportStaleClasses`, wired into `check`) and Phase 8 removes them.
- ⚠️ **`Stream`'s out-of-range reads fail silently instead of throwing** — a desync hazard. ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY KEPT as of 2026-10-04, NOT a Phase 2 target** (see 2.3): these accessors read every packet field and every cache definition, so changing what an out-of-range read returns would change how a desync presents, which the frozen-contract rule forbids. `readUnsignedWord()` wraps its body in `catch (Exception e) { return readUnsignedWord2(); }`, and `readUnsignedWord2()` is a **hardcoded `return 1795;`** (`readUnsignedByte2()` likewise returns a hardcoded `-263`, and `method421()` returns `-1` on any exception). So reading past the end of the buffer does not raise an error — it yields a plausible-looking wrong number, which is harder to diagnose than a crash. Two assertions in `ClientHarness` pin the **current** behaviour as a tripwire, so any future change to the sentinel has to be deliberate and has to move them with it.

---

## The rule this repo must keep (mirrors `.cursor/rules/refactor-progress.mdc`)

- At the start of every task, read the "Current status" block above and continue from "Next step".
- After each completed step, update that block: "Last updated", "Last thing I did", "Next step", and any counts you verified.
- Before stopping mid-task, update the block first.
- Edit only the status block and the relevant phase section. Never rewrite the rest of the file.
- **Never mark a step done until it has been verified** — and for this client that means *verified on a live client against the real server*, not merely "it compiles".
- **Flag a phase the moment it is blocked or contradicted**, in the phase section and in the status block. A phase that turns out to be wrong is more useful recorded than silently dropped.

---

## The one non-negotiable: compatibility with the server

The user's stated highest priority. Restated precisely so it is testable rather than a feeling:

**The client must always be able to log into and play the *current, unmodified* server.** Concretely, the following are **frozen contracts** and every phase must leave them byte-identical unless the phase is explicitly about them:

| Contract | Where it lives | Why it breaks compatibility |
|---|---|---|
| **Packet opcodes and their payload layouts** | `client.java`'s incoming-packet switch, and `Stream` read helpers | A reordered read desyncs the stream and every later packet is garbage. This is the single most dangerous surface in the whole plan. |
| **Login handshake** (ISAAC seeds, `CreateUID`/UKEY, the login block) | `client.java`, `ISAACRandomGen`, `CreateUID` | A mismatch = "invalid login" with no useful error. |
| **Cache format and indices** (474 cache; archive/file numbering; `Skins.dat`, `Frames.dat`, loose `*.dat` frames) | `StreamLoader`, `Decompressor`, `Class36`, `OnDemandFetcher` | The client reads what the server/cache serves. Renumbering a single index silently loads the wrong model. |
| **On-demand fetching** (`OnDemandFetcher` requests vs what the server answers) | `OnDemandFetcher` | Desyncs model/anim loading mid-game. |
| **Object collision footprints** | `ObjectCollisionSizes` (generated) vs `Data/objectSize.cfg` | Client and server must agree or players clip through/path into scenery inconsistently. |

**Consequences for how this plan is written:**
- ⚠️ **Java version is *not* on this list, deliberately.** It cannot be — separate JVMs. Do not let the version upgrade be treated as risky to the server.
- ⚠️ **No phase may "tidy up" a packet read.** Refactors of `client.java` must be *purely mechanical and behaviour-preserving* (rename/move), with the packet switch treated as frozen code that moves but never changes.
- ⚠️ **Cache-reading changes are the other frozen surface.** Phase 3 *adds bounds checks around* reads; it does not change what is read or in what order.
- The client must be regression-tested against the **same server build** before and after each phase, using the Phase 0 golden-master baseline.

### ⚠️ The contract was missing a whole layer: this is NOT a pure 474 client

**Added 2026-10-04 after the user revealed that the client already carries 667-revision content and is meant to carry more.** The table above treats "the cache format" as one frozen thing. It is not. The client is a **474 base with 667 (and 647) data injected on top**, through at least four different ad-hoc mechanisms, each relying on an **undocumented ID-avoidance convention** so the injections cannot clobber 474 content:

| Injection | Mechanism | Avoiding-collision convention |
|---|---|---|
| **667 NPCs** — `Nex` (ids **13447+**), `Max` (650), `Callisto`, `Blood reaver` | **Hardcoded `switch` in `EntityDef`** (471 lines, 10 cases) with literal model/anim ids | Uses **high NPC ids** so 474 npc ids are untouched; custom models remapped into the **90xxx** range (`252 → 90252`) |
| **667 curse seq/spotanim + models** | `CurseData667` reads a `CursePack/` folder under the cache (`seq.dat`, `spotanim.dat`, `anims/*.gz`, `models/*.gz`) | Frame file ids remapped into **Class36 slots 28000+** so curse frames cannot overwrite whip/godsword/run frames that share the original 667 file ids |
| **667/647 frame archives** | `Class36.loadFrames()` also **auto-loads any loose numeric `{id}.dat` in the cache root with id ≥ 100** (`load_647`), e.g. Nex's `3502.dat` | Implicit: filename must be purely numeric and ≥100 |
| **667 definition readers** | `SpotAnim.readValues667` coexists with the 474 `readValues`; `CurseData667` hand-replicates 667 opcode skipping (`skipSequence`/`skipSpotAnim`) | Selecting only wanted ids and **skipping the rest by re-implementing the 667 format** |

**Why this materially changes the plan:**
- ⚠️ **The "cache format is frozen" row was incomplete.** There are four data sources with three different collision-avoidance schemes. A refactor that changes `Class36`'s slot allocation, model-loading order, or the `EntityDef` id range can **silently clobber 474 content** — a bug that looks like "some player's godsword animation is wrong now", with no crash and no error.
- ⚠️ **The remapping conventions are load-bearing but nowhere documented in code as a contract.** `ANIM_FILE_BASE = 28000` and the `90xxx` model range are magic numbers with a comment each, not an invariant anything enforces. Phase 8's "remove superseded code" is the obvious place someone deletes one by accident.
- ⚠️ **Phase 6 (animation preservation) is bigger and riskier than written.** It says "474 frames/skins", but the frame space is *shared* between 474 packed data, loose 647 archives, and the 28000+ curse remap. Phase 6.1 must document **all four sources and the slot budget** before touching anything.
- ⚠️ **Phase 3 (cache readers) must preserve the injection paths**, including `CurseData667`'s hand-written 667 opcode skippers, which are frozen *by the same logic as the packet switch* — they encode an external format.
- ✅ **Good news for the user's stated direction:** adding *more* 667 data via these conventions does **not** touch the wire protocol, so it does not threaten server compatibility. The risk is internal (474 content integrity), which is a much easier thing to test with the Phase 0.4 harness.

### The 474/634 chooser is dead UI — worth knowing before it misleads anyone

`Loader` shows "474 characters" / "**634** characters" and passes `"474"` or `"667"` to `client.main`. ⚠️ **Every part of that is inert:**
- The label says **634** but the code passes **667** — they disagree.
- `client.main(args)` forwards to `new Jframe(args)`, and **`Jframe`'s constructor never reads `args`**. The argument is dropped on the floor.

**So both radio buttons launch a byte-identical client, and the revision label is decorative.** Not a bug to fix now (and *not* something a refactor should silently "correct", since changing it changes behaviour), but it must not be mistaken for a real 474/667 switch when reasoning about the data model — the 667 content is always present.

## Reference review (what I actually found, not what the brief assumed)

### `ubjelly/317-OpenGL` — ⚠️ not usable
The entire repository is **three files**: `src/org/derithium/Client.java` (1,647 B), `Game.java` (215 B), `ImageProducer.java` (864 B), plus compiled copies in `bin/`. There is no renderer, no shader, no GL code — it is an abandoned skeleton of a structure (`org.derithium`) with nothing in it. **Nothing can be learned from it beyond "someone once intended a `Game`/`ImageProducer` split".** Do not spend further time on it.

### `Elvarg-Community/Elvarg-Client-Public` — the real reference, but **not portable**
This is a **full RuneLite client** (Gradle Kotlin build; **1,156** Java files: **1,085** under `net/runelite/`, **209** under `com/runescape/`; "Current Data: 206"; README lists Textures, Model Class, HD, GPU, RuneLite as its features). Relevant architecture:

- `net/runelite/client/plugins/gpu/GpuPlugin.java` — RuneLite's official GPU plugin; `Shader.java`, `GpuFloatBuffer.java`, `GpuIntBuffer.java`.
- `net/runelite/client/plugins/hd/…` — the HD plugin: `model/ModelPusher.java`, `model/ModelHasher.java`, `model/ModelCache.java`, `opengl/shader/Shader.java`, `utils/ModelHash.java`.
- `com/runescape/entity/model/` — clean `Model`, `ModelHeader`, `ModelLoader`, `Renderable`.
- `com/runescape/cache/anim/Frame.java`, `FrameBase.java` — the animation transform structures.

⚠️ **It cannot be dropped in or cherry-picked wholesale.** It is a RuneLite client: named packages, a mixin/API layer, a plugin host, and a different cache/data revision. Our client is a **flat default-package 317/474 client of 131 files and 59,694 lines**. The value is as a **technique blueprint** — specifically its *seam*: it renders the 3D scene through GL and then composites the software-drawn UI over the top, rather than trying to GPU everything.

### Verdict on the user's three suggested prompts

- **Prompt 1 (GpuRenderer + LWJGL window over the native frame hook)** — ✅ **the abstract-`GpuRenderer` idea is right, ❌ the "bind an LWJGL display over the client's native frame hook" part is the known dead end.** `Display.setParent` was already attempted in this exact codebase and abandoned for deadlocking Windows (see `LwjglPresent`). Phase 6–7 keep the abstraction but use **LWJGL 3's `AWTGLCanvas`** (a single AWT component, the supported path) or a headless offscreen context — **never `Display.setParent`**.
- **Prompt 2 (Model → vertex arrays / VBO)** — ✅ correct and safe **as an additive phase**. Phase 5 exposes geometry; it must not alter the software rasteriser's output.
- **Prompt 3 (474 animation transforms must survive GPU rendering)** — ✅ correct and important, and the resolution is the one RuneLite actually uses: **apply the per-frame transform on the CPU (as `Model.method443`/`Animable` already do) and upload the transformed vertices**, rather than moving skinning into a shader. Our frames are `Class36` (`Skins.dat`, `Frames.dat`, loose frame files, plus `load_647`) and skins are `Class18`. See Phase 6.

### The LWJGL lesson, recorded once so it is not relearned
- `deps/lwjgl.jar` is **LWJGL 2.x** (`org/lwjgl/WindowsSysImplementation`, `LWJGLUtil`, `BufferChecks`) and is **imported by zero files**.
- ⚠️ **`Display.setParent` + AWT = deadlock on Windows.** Both toolkits pump the same HWND. This is why `LwjglPresent` is a stub.
- **The recommendation for Phase 7 is LWJGL 3** (`org.lwjgl.opengl.awt.AWTGLCanvas`), which supports Java 8+ and has a supported AWT embedding, and is a different design from LWJGL 2 rather than an upgrade of it.

---

## Architecture as it stands (the baseline this plan must not break)

- **Entry:** `Loader` (Swing chooser: *474 characters* vs *634 characters*) → `client.main(new String[]{"474"})`.
- **Shell:** `RSApplet extends Applet implements Runnable` + `RSFrame`; full window/listener/input plumbing.
- **Game loop:** `RSApplet.run()` — the classic 317 loop (`delayTime = 20` → 50 logic ticks/s via `processGameLoop`, `minDelay = 1`), **plus an `fpsUnlocked` uncapped branch** (`while (now - lastLogicTime >= 20 && ticks < 8)`) that draws every frame. Rendering is `processDrawing()`.
- **Renderer:** pure **software** — `RSImageProducer` wraps `int[]` pixels; `DrawingArea` does primitives; `Texture.java` (2,016 lines) does software texture mapping; **`Model.java` (2,823 lines) is the software rasteriser** (`method443` applies transform, `method478` scale, `method476` recolour, `method479` lighting).
- **Present:** `Graphics.drawImage` of the framebuffer, optionally through Java2D's GL pipeline (`GlPresent`).
- **Scene:** `WorldController` (2,548), `ObjectManager`, `Ground`.
- **Big files:** `client.java` **20,129** lines; `Model` 2,823; `WorldController` 2,548; `Texture` 2,016; `RSInterface` 1,722; `Sprite` 1,111; `OnDemandFetcher` 629; `Class11` 571.
- **Total:** 131 files, **59,694** lines, flat default package, no tests, no build system.
