Fixing the oracle is now a precondition of this step rather than a follow-up to it.** ⚠️ **Do not loosen the gate to make it pass — that is the vacuous-check failure this plan keeps finding.** ✅ **THEN THE CONTROL RUN SETTLED IT (same session): comparing the three post-rename captures against EACH OTHER — all three are the current binary — gives `221412` vs `221551` = COMPATIBLE (1,100-unit prefix, LCS 97.5%) but `221412` vs `221811` and `221551` vs `221811` = DRIFT SUSPECTED (17-unit prefix, LCS 98.1% and 96.8%).** ⚠️⚠️ **The two pairs it FAILS are more similar than the pair it PASSES, so the positional prefix gate is the sole discriminator and the verdict is a proven false positive — the same binary cannot drift from itself.** ⚠️ **Cause confirmed byte-for-byte on all three: units 17-18 are a length-prefixed String, `R 2 0006` + `R 6 "98%\n"` in the two that pass against `R 2 0007` + `R 7 "100%\n"` in `221811`, so the length prefix itself changes and breaks the match at two units.** ⚠️ **Consequence for this step: the blocker is now REMOVED rather than merely located — the comparator is repaired and all three post-rename captures return COMPATIBLE, so 3.3's live evidence exists and is readable.** ⚠️ **What 3.3 still lacks is the screen recording, which the packet oracle cannot stand in for.**

### Phase 3.2 close-out

- **Seams taken: 6 of 6 planned.** `ClientSettings` (settings/options), `Camera` (camera/view), `LoginScreen` (login screen), `TabArea` (tab bar), `ChatArea` (chat), `MenuSystem` (right-click menus).
- **`client.java`: 20,996 -> 16,397 lines** (4,599 lines, 21.9%, moved out into six cohesive classes).
- **Every seam live-verified on both axes** - `Record.bat` `compareCapture` COMPATIBLE *and* user-confirmed render/interaction - against the unmodified server. Prefix stayed 595-599/600 and LCS 95-98% on every run.
- **The reconnaissance table is empty.** No seventh cohesive cluster was found; the remaining coupling is the shared hub (`myPlayer`, `stream`, `menuAction*`, `drawInterface`, the generic geometry helpers).
- **Hand off to Phase 4 (Renderer abstraction)**, not to a 3.2.7. The hub state getting a real owner is a separate, later concern.

### Phase 4 — Renderer abstraction (no behaviour change)
- [ ] **4.1 Define a `GpuRenderer` seam** around the two things that will change: the **scene/model rasterisation** and the **present**. Software implementation only; it must produce output **byte-identical to today** (verified by hashing the framebuffer for a fixed scene, mirroring Phase 0.2's hashing approach).
- [ ] **4.2 Make the renderer selectable at runtime** behind a setting, defaulting to software. ⚠️ Reuse the existing `client_settings.properties` mechanism that `GlPresent` already reads, rather than inventing a second config path.
  - ⚠️ **SPLIT (2026-10-05) into 4.2a and 4.2b, because the two halves have different risk and only the first is structural.** See *Phase 4.2 SCOPED* below.
- [x] **4.3 Prove the seam is behaviour-neutral** with the golden master *and* a framebuffer hash. No GL code is written in this phase. ✅ **DONE 2026-10-05 - but the claim is deliberately NARROWER than "byte-identical scene output"; see *Phase 4.3 DONE* below.**

#### Phase 4.1 SCOPED (2026-10-05) - measured against the tree at `83e56c77`

**Headline: 4.1 is two halves of very different size, and the plan's own wording treats them as one. The present half is ALREADY a seam; the rasterisation half is the whole job.**

**Half 1 - the present: already abstracted, ~5 lines of real code.**
- Every write to the AWT surface funnels through **one** method: `RSImageProducer.drawGraphics(int, Graphics, int)` -> `GlPresent.blit(...)` -> `Graphics2D.drawImage`, and `blit()` already pins the deterministic hints (`INTERPOLATION_NEAREST_NEIGHBOR`, AA off, `RENDER_SPEED`).
- `client.drawGameBuffer()` (the game frame) and `client.blitTitle()` (login) are the only two wrappers, and `drawGameBuffer()` already tries a GPU hook first: `if (GlPresent.presentGame(...)) return;`.
- **Both GPU present hooks are dead stubs:** `GlPresent.presentGame(...)` and `LwjglPresent.present(...)` `return false` unconditionally, and `LwjglPresent.isSupported()` is `false`. ⚠️ **Do not build 4.1 on these - Phase 8.2 already lists them for deletion.** The seam should be defined fresh, or the stubs formalised deliberately, not inherited by accident.
- ⚠️ **`openGlEnabled` already exists and is NOT the seam 4.2 describes.** `GlPresent.applyPipeline()` reads `openGl` from `client_settings.properties` and sets `sun.java2d.opengl` system properties - that selects **Java2D's own pipeline**, not a renderer of ours. So 4.2's "selectable at runtime behind a setting" is *half*-present, and the config path genuinely is the one to reuse (as the plan says) - but it currently toggles something else, so the naming must not be conflated.

**Half 2 - the rasterisation: ~3,700 lines, and the coupling is a `static` global.**
- The software rasteriser is spread across `WorldController` (2,720 lines; scene: `method310` projection setup 87, `method313` per-tick render 186, `method314` ground/tiles 465, `method319` 131, `method321` 102, `method305-309`, `method315/316`, `method323/324`), `Model` (2,949 lines; triangles: `method443`, `method472` 163, `method479` 97, `method483` 177, `method485` 171, `method480`, `method482`, `method484`, `method486`), and `Texture` (2,096 lines; textured spans `method375/377/379`, ~50 direct sites).
- ⚠️ **The real substrate is a public `static` global, not a parameter:** `DrawingArea.pixels[]`, `.width`, `.height`, plus 7 clip/centre statics. `WorldController`, `Model`, `Texture`, `Sprite` and `TextDrawingArea` all reach into `DrawingArea.pixels` statically - `Texture` alone does so at ~50 sites. **That static global is what the seam has to capture**, and capturing it is a larger and riskier change than "define an interface".
- The three entry points are few and named: `WorldController.method310(500, 800, viewW, viewH, ai)` (called twice, `client.java:985` and `8332`), `worldController.method313(...)` (called at `client.java:15657`), and `Model.method443(...)` (called ~25x, all inside `WorldController.method313`).

**The precondition the plan does not yet have - and it must come FIRST.**
- 4.1's stated gate is *"hashing the framebuffer for a fixed scene, mirroring Phase 0.2's hashing approach"*. ⚠️ **Phase 0.2 hashed FILES (`BIN-BASELINE.sha256`), not framebuffers, so this gate does not exist yet.** A framebuffer hash also needs a **fixed scene**, which means either a headless deterministic render or a recorded replay - neither exists today.
- **So building the framebuffer-hash gate is a precondition of 4.1, not a follow-up to it.** This repo's hardest-won lesson is that a green build once stood while the canvas was white: *"it compiles" is not evidence*, and for a renderer refactor the framebuffer hash is the only cheap objective oracle we can have. Without it, 4.1's "byte-identical output" claim would be unfalsifiable.

**Recommended staging (each its own live-gated step, no GL code anywhere in this phase):**
- **4.1a - formalise the present seam. DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05).** `ui/Renderer.java` is now the single named present seam: `Renderer.blit(producer, g, destX, destY)` for the sub-region path (minimap, chat, tab, login, fixed-mode frame pieces) and `Renderer.presentGameFrame(producer, g, destX, destY)` for the game frame, which keeps the pre-existing GPU hook and fallback exactly as `drawGameBuffer` performed them. **All 12 call sites routed:** 9 in `client.java` (`blitTitle`, `drawGameBuffer`, `fullGameScreen`, the four fixed-mode frame pieces, 2x minimap), 2 in `ChatArea`, 1 in `TabArea`. ⚠️ **The seam is ENFORCED rather than documented: `RSImageProducer.drawGraphics` is now package-private, so it has zero callers outside `Renderer` and a reintroduced direct blit fails to compile** (verified by grep: only `Renderer.java` and the declaration itself reference it). ⚠️ **The hazard this step carried was a COORDINATE SWAP and nothing else** - `drawGraphics` is **y-first** (`drawGraphics(destY, g, destX)`) while `Renderer.blit` is **x-first**, so all 12 conversions were swaps no compiler can check; each was verified by hand against the original (`rightFrame.drawGraphics(4, g, 516)` -> `blit(rightFrame, g, 516, 4)`) and then adjudicated by the live screen. **Live gate:** `Record.bat` vs the unmodified server -> **COMPATIBLE, prefix 597/600, LCS 98.4%, exit 0** -> user confirmed everything in the right place. **Static gates:** compile clean, `clean check installBin` green, harness **46/46**, warnings **100** (baseline), cfg in sync, no lints, source diff **only the present path** (4 files, 20+/16-, +1 new).
- **4.1b - build the framebuffer-hash gate. DONE AND MUTATION-TESTED (2026-10-05).** Added a raster section to `test/ClientHarness.java` (**46 -> 49 assertions, all passing**) that runs a fixed deterministic workload through the real primitives and pins the framebuffer by SHA-256. Workload: `initDrawingArea`, `setAllPixels`, 16x `drawHorizontalLine`, the alpha-blend path (`method335`), a `Sprite` blit, a sub-region blit, and a clipped pass (`setDrawingArea`/`defaultDrawingAreaSize`) so the clip maths is pinned too. Three assertions: the **pinned golden hash** (`775ffc78...a9e39e`), **determinism** (two runs agree exactly), and **teeth** (one altered draw op changes the hash). ⚠️⚠️ **Mutation-tested against the REAL rasteriser, not just in the harness: changing `DrawingArea.method335`'s alpha blend one bit (`>> 8` -> `>> 7` on red) made the pinned-hash test FAIL and the harness exit 1; reverting restored 49/49.** ⚠️ **The hash was confirmed stable across THREE separate JVM runs before pinning**, since per-run hash ordering is how such a gate goes flaky. ⚠️ **Scope boundary, stated in the harness doc rather than implied: covers the DRAWING SUBSTRATE (`DrawingArea` + `Sprite`), NOT `Model`/`WorldController` geometry**, which needs a cache fixture the repo lacks. ⚠️ **Safeguard kept: if the constant is reset to zeros the test prints the observed value and FAILS** rather than passing quietly. **Diff: one file, zero production code**, so no live session applies. **Static gates:** compile clean, `clean check installBin` green, harness **49/49**, warnings **100**.
- **4.1c - introduce the rasterisation seam.** Split into sub-steps, because this is the largest change in the plan.
  - **4.1c-1 DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05) - the substrate is now `ui/Framebuffer.java`.** Extracted the 2D substrate out of `DrawingArea` into a new superclass `ui/Framebuffer.java` (**417 lines**: `pixels`/`width`/`height`, the 7 clip/centre statics, and **all 18 primitives** - `initDrawingArea`, `setDrawingArea`, `setAllPixels`, `drawHorizontalLine`, `drawPixels`, `fillPixels`, `method335`/`336`/`338`/`339`/`340`/`341`/`342`, `drawLine`, `blendHLine`, `plot`; the 3 originally-private ones stayed private), leaving **`DrawingArea` a 27-line subclass** (`DrawingArea extends Framebuffer extends NodeSub`). ⚠️⚠️ **Design choice that made it land clean: an EXTRACT-SUPERCLASS, not an extract-and-repoint.** Static members are inherited in Java, so every existing reference keeps resolving - `DrawingArea.width`/`DrawingArea.pixels`, AND the unqualified inherited form inside `Sprite`/`Texture`/`TextDrawingArea`/`Background` (all `extend DrawingArea`) - so the **~570 field reads across 17 files needed no edit at all**. ⚠️ **Two proofs rather than one: the compiler was clean on the FIRST try (what a true no-call-site move predicts), and a grep audit shows only `DrawingArea.java` modified + `Framebuffer.java` added with ZERO files referencing `Framebuffer.` directly.** ⚠️⚠️ **The 4.1b gate earned its keep: the framebuffer hash is UNMOVED (`775ffc78...a9e39e`) after 395 lines and 10 mutable statics moved.** ⚠️ **Boundary stated plainly: `Framebuffer` still holds STATIC state, so this NAMES the rasteriser but does not make it SWAPPABLE** - the ownership move is 4.1c-2. **Live gate:** `Record.bat` vs the unmodified server -> **COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600 (strongest of the refactor), LCS 96.7%, exit 0** -> user confirmed it rendered **exactly as before**. **Static gates:** compile clean first try, `clean check installBin` green, harness **49/49**, warnings **100**, cfg in sync.
  - **✅ 4.1c-2 RESOLVED WITHOUT THE OWNERSHIP MOVE (2026-10-05) - and this is the plan's most useful correction so far.** The original item was "take `Framebuffer`'s static buffer and give it an owner so a GPU path can substitute its own storage" - ~570 references, the largest churn in the plan. ⚠️ **Scoping showed a GPU path does not need it: a GPU renderer intercepts GEOMETRY SUBMISSION, not storage, so the seam belongs at the operations (`method443`, `method315`/`method316`) rather than at the buffer.** It landed instead as three operation seams - **4.1c-2a** (the gate), **4.1c-2b** (models) and **4.1c-2c** (ground) - with **zero** call sites changed and the buffer untouched. ⚠️ **So do not conflate 4.1c-1 with 4.1c-2: the substrate is still STATIC and still not swappable, and that is now a deliberate decision rather than an unfinished step.** If a later phase genuinely needs offscreen/multi-target rendering, THAT is the moment to reconsider - justified by a concrete requirement, not by the seam's existence.

#### Phase 4.1c-2 SCOPED (2026-10-05) - and the recommendation is to NOT do the ownership move

**Measured size of the ownership move, against the tree at `5ed33f84`:**
- **842 QUALIFIED references** to the seam (556 field reads + 286 method calls) via `DrawingArea.X`, plus roughly **50 UNQUALIFIED inherited** uses that no grep can find reliably - `Sprite.java` ~30 and `RSFont.java` ~20 are the only subclasses with any; `Texture`, `TextDrawingArea` and `Background` use only the qualified form. Seven classes extend `DrawingArea`/`Framebuffer` (`Texture`, `Background`, `DrawingArea`, `rsDrawingArea`, `RSFont`, `Sprite`, `TextDrawingArea`), so an instance-owned buffer would have to reach all seven.
- Moving the buffer to instance ownership therefore means rewriting ~900 references across 17 files and changing the base class of seven types - **the largest mechanical change in the plan, and it is pure churn: no behaviour may change, so all of it is risk without output.**

**The finding that changes the recommendation: a GPU path needs OPERATION dispatch, not STORAGE ownership.**
- The plan framed 4.1's second half as *"the scene/model rasterisation"*, and 4.1c then drifted into treating the **buffer** as the thing to make swappable. Those are different axes, and only one of them is on the GPU path.
- A GL renderer intercepts **triangle submission** and draws to its own target. The software path keeps writing to `DrawingArea.pixels`, which remains the correct storage for the software implementation. Nothing about a GPU path requires that buffer to stop being static - it requires that the rasteriser's **operations can be routed elsewhere**.
- **And the operation surface is tiny: `Model.method443` - the triangle rasteriser entry - has only 22 call sites - 21 of them inside a single method (`WorldController.method314`) plus one delegating wrapper (`Animable.method443`).** That is the real seam, and it is ~40x smaller than the ownership move.
- ⚠️ **The architecture already agrees: the buffer is a "current target" pointer, not owned state.** Multiple buffers already exist as separate `RSImageProducer` instances, and `DrawingArea.initDrawingArea` rebinds the statics to whichever is current (only 6 call sites). Instance-owning the buffer would fight that design rather than clarify it.

**Recommended 4.1c-2: define the seam at the OPERATION level, and record the rejected alternative.**
- **4.1c-2a DONE (2026-10-05) - the triangle rasteriser is now gated.** Added three assertions to `ClientHarness` driving **`Texture.method374`** (the flat triangle rasteriser that `Model.method443` delegates to) with fixed integer coordinates, pinned by SHA-256 at `57838498b8af2b9c834b7a82bcd37305712f1014665926449b3aaa9c88732d67`. ⚠️ **Why this was the precondition rather than busywork:** 4.1b covers `DrawingArea` + `Sprite` only, so before this every change under `method443` was gated by nothing but a human looking at the screen. **Three findings that only running it produced:** (1) colours are **16-BIT model face colours** indexing a 65536-entry palette - passing 24-bit RGB throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: 65643`; (2) `Texture.method364()` is the required setup (row offsets + texture centres from the current `DrawingArea` size); (3) ⚠️⚠️ **`Texture.method372` cannot be used to build the palette because it adds `Math.random() * 0.03 - 0.015` - the client's palette is deliberately jittered per load** - so the harness fills the palette deterministically instead. ⚠️ **`Fog.sceneDepth` is deliberately set to 0 so `method374`'s fog guard short-circuits, which keeps the gate from loading the `client` class**, whose static initialisers are not headless-safe. **Verified: deterministic across two separate JVM runs; teeth on its own input; and mutation-tested against the REAL rasteriser** (a one-bit change to the pixel write in `Texture.method375` failed it). Harness **49 -> 52/52**, warnings **100**, `src` restored byte-clean. ⚠️ **Gap stated plainly: the FLAT path only.** `method376`/`method378` (textured) need the texture pixel tables and are not yet driven.
- **4.1c-2a EXTENDED (2026-10-05) - the gate now covers the TEXTURED triangle path too, which was the 4.1c-2c precondition.** Added three assertions driving **`Texture.method378`**, pinned at `018753a038c2c05a895f7c341a176a8f77436093f7c5d419df22154fd721efe1`. ⚠⚠ **Necessary, not decorative: the ground reaches `method378` at 5 of its 11 rasteriser call sites, and that path is live in normal play (`client.java:3812` sets `Texture.lowMem = false` under high detail), so without this it was covered by the live screen alone.** **Three findings from running it: (1) `method376` is NOT textured - it is a flat `method377` variant, and it is undrivable headlessly because it calls `Fog.applyFlat` unconditionally, which reads `client.fogStrength`; `method378` is the textured one and its fog use is guarded.** **(2) No cache or `Background` is needed: `method371` returns early when `anIntArrayArray1479[tid] != null`, so a synthetic texture parked in that private slot drives the real span routine - reached by REFLECTION rather than by widening production visibility.** **(3) The first mutation attempt went UNDETECTED, and that is the valuable result: a parked texture never sets `aBooleanArray1475[tid]`, so the executed branch is the sampled `aBoolean1463 == true` path, not the `ai[k] = k8` solid branch at L1830.** **Mutation-proved: `^ 1` on the sampled write at `Texture.java:1777` failed the pin.** Harness **56 -> 59/59**, warnings **100**, `Texture.java` restored byte-clean. ⚠ **`method376` remains un-gated (it is `client`-dependent).**
- **4.1c-2b DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05) - `ui/SceneRasterizer.java`: the scene MODEL seam.** Hooked at the TOP of `Model.method443` as an early dispatch (`if (SceneRasterizer.dispatch(...)) return;`) - **5 added lines, 0 deletions in `Model.java`, and ZERO call sites changed.** ⚠️ **Why one hook rather than the 21 sites in `method314`: `method443` is itself the per-model submission point, so an early dispatch covers every caller, whereas a missed call site would silently keep a software-only path.** Mirrors 4.1a's ask-`GlPresent`-then-fall-through pattern. ⚠️ **Neutrality measured, not argued: the rasteriser sources (`Texture`, `DrawingArea`, `Framebuffer`, `WorldController`) are byte-clean and BOTH pinned hashes are unmoved.** The 4 new assertions prove the seam is inert by default AND that an installed stub intercepts `method443` - the latter only satisfiable if the hook sits above the method's culling, since `Model.aModel_1621` would otherwise return early, so the test pins WHERE the hook is. **Live gate:** `Record.bat` vs the unmodified server -> **COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 99.0% (highest of the refactor), exit 0** -> user confirmed every model rendered exactly as before. **Static gates:** harness **52 -> 56/56**, `clean check installBin` green, warnings **100**. ⚠️ **NOT claimed: the GROUND is not covered** - `method316` bypasses `method443` entirely; that is 4.1c-2c.

#### Phase 4.1c-2b SCOPED (2026-10-05) - and the design changes ZERO call sites

**The surface, measured:** **21** `method443` calls live in `WorldController.method314` - ⚠️ **corrected: an earlier draft of this section said `method313`, which is the queue/culling method (decl L955) that CALLS `method314` (decl L1142, the per-tile scene drawer); the plan's own reconnaissance at L331 already had this right, so the error was mine** - plus **one delegating wrapper at `Animable.java:18`**, and every one has the SAME shape - `<model>.method443(orientation, camA, camB, camC, camD, dx, dy, dz, uid)`. The four shared arguments are the current plane's **camera sin/cos rotation** (`anInt458..461` = `Model.modelIntArray1/2[i1]`, set once per pass at `:858-861`), and the three after them are the object's position minus the camera origin.

**Why this is one hook and not 22 edits.** `method443` is ITSELF the per-model submission point - it loops over the model's own triangles internally (`anIntArray1637`, `anIntArray1640`, ...), so one call = "draw this model, placed here". The seam therefore goes at the TOP of `method443` as a 3-line early dispatch, exactly mirroring the pattern 4.1a already established for the present:
```java
if (SceneRasterizer.dispatch(this, i, j, k, l, i1, j1, k1, l1, i2)) return;
// ... existing body, untouched ...
```
⚠️ **This is strictly safer than editing the 22 call sites, and it is the same reasoning that made 4.1c-1 clean:** a call site left behind would silently keep a software-only path, whereas an early dispatch covers every caller now and in future. The software path is not moved, not wrapped and not renamed - the body is untouched - so **the 4.1c-2a hash must stay unmoved, and that is the proof the step is behaviour-neutral.**

**Placement and dependency direction:** new `ui/SceneRasterizer.java` (interface + installable implementation + a static `dispatch` returning `boolean`, like `GlPresent.presentGame`). ⚠️ **It goes in `ui` rather than `scene` or `model` because the direction `model -> ui` ALREADY EXISTS** (`model.Texture` uses `ui.DrawingArea`), so this adds no new package coupling; putting it in `model` would also work but `ui` matches where the other two rendering seams (`Renderer`, `GlPresent`) already live.

⚠️⚠️ **THE HONEST GAP, AND IT IS BIGGER THAN THE SEAM: the GROUND DOES NOT GO THROUGH `method443`.** the ground is drawn by **TWO** methods, bypassing `Model.method443` entirely: **`method315`** (decl L1608; mesh `Class43` = `Ground.aClass43_1311`, the floor) with **7** rasteriser calls, and **`method316`** (decl L1899; mesh `Class40` = `aClass40_1312`, the per-tile overlay) with **4**. ⚠️ **An earlier draft said "`method316` draws the landscape with 11 direct calls" - wrong on BOTH counts: the 11 is the two methods combined, and `method316` alone has 4.** So a seam at `method443` covers **models only**: objects, walls, roofs, NPCs and players, but **NOT the floor the player walks on**. A GPU path needs both, so:
- **✅ 4.1c-2c DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05) - the scene rasteriser is now seamed: MODELS **and** THE GROUND.** Added a second `SceneRasterizer` operation, **`drawGroundTriangle(...)`**, plus `dispatchGroundTriangle(...)` which declines when nothing is installed, and hooked it in **THREE** places: `WorldController.method315` (the `Class43` floor mesh) has TWO triangle blocks, `WorldController.method316` (the `Class40` overlay mesh) has one. ⚠⚠ **Correction to my own scope, found while implementing: I said "one hook per drawer, so two" - it is THREE decision points, because `method315` draws a QUAD as two separate triangles, each with its own picking and its own 3-way branch.** ⚠⚠ **Where this differs from 4.1c-2b, and the reason it is the riskier of the two: the hooks are MID-LOOP, not at the method top. Unlike `method443`, both drawers interleave tile picking (`method318`, setting `anInt470/471` and `hoverTileX/Y`) with drawing inside the same loop, so an early return at the top would SILENTLY DROP tile markers and hover as soon as a rasteriser was installed.** All three hooks sit **AFTER** the picking. ⚠️ **The operation carries the variant instead of hiding it: `textureId == -1` = untextured, `flatMesh` is the mesh's flat flag (which selects the texture-coordinate ordering exactly as the software path's two `method378` shapes do), and `Texture.lowMem` remains globally readable so the low-detail shaded variant is disambiguated too.** ⚠️⚠️ **Neutrality measured: `WorldController` raw diff is 59 insertions / 40 deletions, but `git diff -w` is 19 insertions and ZERO deletions - the 40 are purely the re-indentation of the wrapped chain, so every rasteriser call and branch is byte-identical - with `Model`/`Texture`/`DrawingArea`/`Framebuffer` byte-clean and BOTH pinned hashes unmoved.** ⚠️ **My first insertion attempt produced mangled indentation and I reverted it rather than commit it, which is precisely why the whitespace-ignoring diff is the figure to quote.** The 4 new assertions pin that the seam is inert by default, that an installed stub intercepts, and that the full 19-argument payload arrives in order; **mutation-proved: making the dispatch decline even when installed fails 2 of them.** **Live gate:** `Record.bat` vs the unmodified server -> **COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 98.8%, exit 0** -> user confirmed the ground identical **and** tile markers/hover identical. **Static gates:** harness **59 -> 63/63**, `clean check installBin` green, warnings **100**. ⚠️⚠️ **Honest limit, written into the harness javadoc rather than left implied: hook PLACEMENT is NOT headlessly assertable here** - `method315`/`method316` read `client.tileMarkers` for picking, which loads `client` - so the harness pins the plumbing and argument order while placement rests on reading plus this live gate.

- **Explicitly rejected, with the reason recorded so it is not revisited: the buffer ownership move.** It is the largest churn in the plan and a GPU path does not need it. If a later phase wants offscreen/multi-target rendering, that is the moment to reconsider - and it should be justified by a concrete requirement then, not by the seam's existence now.

- ⚠️ **Honest sizing, stated so it is not discovered late: 4.1c is the largest single change proposed anywhere in this plan** - bigger than any 3.2 seam (the largest, `chat`, was 1,499 lines against a 3,700-line cluster with a static global in the middle). It may need to land in several sub-steps, and the plan should not pretend it is one afternoon's work.


#### Phase 4.2 SCOPED AND SPLIT (2026-10-05) - one seam or two, and where the setting lives

**The decision the plan handed over, and its answer.** 4.1's wording is *"define a `GpuRenderer` seam around the two things that will change: the scene/model rasterisation and the present"*. What 4.1 actually produced is **two seams**, not one: `ui/Renderer.java` (the present) and `ui/SceneRasterizer.java` (the scene). Both work and both are live-verified, but they are separate types with separate install points, so *"a `GpuRenderer` seam"* was two seams wearing one plan item's name. **Decision taken: a thin facade**, because it (a) makes the plan's naming true, (b) gives later phases ONE install point instead of two, and (c) is what a selection setting has to switch anyway. The alternative - keeping them separate and selecting each independently - was rejected as less churn but permanently inaccurate naming with two things to keep in sync.

**Why 4.2a is a facade and not a merge.** It is one field, one forward, one delegated install - no second copy of the software path, no buffering, no GL. `GpuRenderer.Implementation` **`extends SceneRasterizer.Implementation`** rather than restating the scene operations, so the scene half is satisfied by inheritance and cannot drift out of step with the scene interface.

**Why the single install point is ENFORCED.** `SceneRasterizer.install` is now package-private, so `GpuRenderer.install` is the only install path a caller outside `ui` has, and a second independent install cannot reappear by accident - the same enforcement `RSImageProducer.drawGraphics` already provides for the present seam. The harness installs through the facade too, so the tests exercise the real path rather than a side door. ⚠️ **`install(null)` clears BOTH seams at once**, so a caller cannot leave half a renderer installed.

**The fallback order is unchanged.** `Renderer.presentGameFrame` now asks `GpuRenderer` first, then `GlPresent.presentGame`, then blits in software. ⚠ **The dead `GlPresent` stub is deliberately KEPT so the order stays identical; removing it is Phase 8.2's scoped job, not this step's.**

**Why the sub-region blit is NOT on the facade.** `Renderer.blit` covers the minimap, chat area, tab area, login screen and the fixed-mode frame pieces - AWT-composited overlays rather than the game frame - so a GPU path does not take them over and they take no hook.

**4.2b - the setting, and the trap in it.** Make the renderer selectable at runtime behind a setting, defaulting to software, **reusing `client_settings.properties`** (read by `GlPresent.applyPipeline`, written by `ClientSettings`). ⚠⚠ **Do NOT reuse or conflate the existing `openGl` key / `client.openGlEnabled`: those select Java2D's OWN pipeline via `sun.java2d.opengl` system properties, which is a different thing from selecting a renderer of ours.** 4.2b therefore needs its own key, its own load path and its own write-back, and it must **default to software so that an absent key behaves exactly like today**.

**Deliberately left alone (decision, not oversight).** The remaining unseamed rasteriser entry points - `WorldController.method319`/`method321`/`method305-309`/`method323-324` and `Model`'s own triangle helpers (`method472`, `method479`, `method483`, `method485`, `method480-486`) - are **not** being seamed now. The seam covers scene *submission* (models + ground), which is what a GPU path needs; revisit the rest only if a concrete GPU implementation turns out to require it.

**✅ 4.2a DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05) - `ui/GpuRenderer.java`, the one install point.** Thin facade: one field, one forward, one delegated install, no second copy of the software path and no GL. `GpuRenderer.Implementation extends SceneRasterizer.Implementation`, so the scene half is satisfied by inheritance and cannot drift out of step with the scene interface.

⚠️ **The single-install-point property is ENFORCED, not documented:** `SceneRasterizer.install` is package-private, so `GpuRenderer.install` is the only install path a caller outside `ui` has - verified by grep as ZERO external callers - and a second independent install cannot reappear by accident. Same enforcement `RSImageProducer.drawGraphics` already provides for the present seam. `install(null)` clears BOTH seams at once, so a caller cannot leave half a renderer behind, and the harness installs through the facade so the tests exercise the real path rather than a side door.

**Present fallback order unchanged:** `GpuRenderer.presentGameFrame` -> `GlPresent.presentGame` -> software blit. ⚠ **The dead `GlPresent` stub is deliberately KEPT so the order is identical; removing it is Phase 8.2's scoped job.**

**Live gate:** `Record.bat` vs the unmodified server -> **COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 98.9%, exit 0** -> user confirmed the game frame presented normally with all UI overlays in place, and separately that the login screen (**the deliberately-unchanged sub-region path**) was normal too - the right pair of checks for a change to the present: the edited path plus an unchanged control. **Static gates:** harness **63 -> 68/68**, `clean check installBin` green, warnings **100**, `Renderer` diff **3 code lines + javadoc**.

**Also corrected two javadocs in `SceneRasterizer` that had become false:** the class doc still attributed the 22 `method443` call sites to `WorldController.method313` (they are 21 in `method314` plus a delegating wrapper at `Animable.java:18`) and the "known gap" paragraph still claimed the ground was not covered. Both were the plan's already-corrected errors reappearing in code, which is why they were worth fixing rather than leaving.

**4.2b IMPLEMENTED (2026-10-05) - and the design gap it exposed is the important part.** `ui/RendererConfig.java` reads its own key **`renderer`** (default `software`) and installs through `GpuRenderer.install`; `ClientSettings` writes the key back; `RendererConfig.select()` runs at both startup points (`Loader.main`, `client.preloadOpenGlProperty`). No UI toggle, by decision.

⚠⚠ **The gap: the scene seam treated "installed" as "consumed".** `SceneRasterizer.dispatch` returned `true` whenever an implementation was present, whatever that implementation did - so the "logging-only placeholder" this plan called for would have **swallowed every model and ground triangle and made the world vanish.** The present seam could already decline (`presentGameFrame` returns `boolean`); the scene seam could not, and that asymmetry is what hid the bug until the placeholder was actually written.

**Fix: make the two scene operations return `boolean` and have the dispatchers return that answer.** ⚠ **It needed ZERO call-site edits - `Model.method443` and the three ground hooks already branch on the dispatch return value** - verified mechanically as `Model.java` and `WorldController.java` being UNCHANGED, so the already-live-verified seams are unaffected. All three seams now share one contract: **`true` = handled, `false` = fall through**, the same contract `GlPresent.presentGame` has always had.

**With that contract, the placeholder is safe by construction:** a non-software value installs `RendererConfig.DecliningRenderer`, which declines everything, so the load -> select -> install path is exercised end to end and no pixel changes. ⚠ **The `openGl` trap is guarded by a test asserting the key is not `openGl`** - that key selects Java2D's own pipeline via `sun.java2d.opengl`, a different thing entirely. Absent key, unreadable file and unrecognised value all fall back to software.

**Static gates:** harness **68 -> 75/75**, `clean check installBin` green, warnings **100**. ⚠ **Both decline paths mutation-proved:** restoring consume-always on the model dispatch fails the model assertion; the same on the ground dispatch fails the ground assertion - so neither is vacuous.

✅ **LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05)** on the DEFAULT branch (no config edit) - which is the contract that matters, since the key is normally absent: `Record.bat` vs the unmodified server -> **COMPATIBLE, prefix 598/600, LCS 99.3% (highest of the entire refactor), exit 0** -> user confirmed it rendered exactly as before. ⚠ **Stated rather than glossed: that verifies the default/declined arm. The non-software arm (`renderer=placeholder`) was NOT exercised live, and no static gate can reach it either, so that arm rests on reading plus the decline-contract tests.**

#### Phase 4.3 DONE (2026-10-05) - the neutrality argument, and the hash that does NOT exist

**What 4.3 asks:** prove the seam is behaviour-neutral with the golden master *and* a framebuffer hash. It is the step that closes Phase 4, and it is written here as a four-part argument where **each part is falsifiable** rather than as an assertion.

**(1) By construction.** With no implementation installed, `dispatch`/`dispatchGroundTriangle` return `false` and `presentGameFrame` returns `false`, so every software body runs unmodified - not moved, wrapped or renamed. This is asserted, not assumed: *"dispatch declines when nothing is installed"*, *"the ground seam declines when no rasteriser is installed"*, *"presentGameFrame declines when nothing is installed"*.

**(2) Mechanically, by the diffs.** ⚠ **The sharpest single number is the ground seam's `git diff -w`: 19 insertions / 0 deletions against a raw 59/40** - i.e. the 40 raw deletions are nothing but re-indentation of the wrapped chain, so every rasteriser call and branch survives byte-identical. `Model.method443` was +5/-0. `Renderer.presentGameFrame` was 3 code lines. And when 4.2b changed the dispatch contract, **`Model.java` and `WorldController.java` needed no edit at all**, which is how the earlier verification survives that change.

**(3) Objectively, by the framebuffer hash.** Three pins, each covering a different workload, each mutation-proved against the real code rather than against a mock:

| pin | covers | proved by |
|---|---|---|
| `775ffc78...a9e39e` | the 2D substrate (`DrawingArea` + `Sprite`), Phase 4.1b | `method335` alpha blend one bit off (`>> 8` -> `>> 7`) |
| `57838498...732d67` | the FLAT triangle rasteriser (`Texture.method374`), 4.1c-2a | one altered triangle colour; and the span write |
| `018753a0...21efe1` | the TEXTURED triangle rasteriser (`Texture.method378`), 4.1c-2a ext. | `^ 1` on the sampled write at `Texture.java:1777` |

⚠️ **They are a GATE, not a report: `check` depends on `harness` (`build.gradle:173`), so `gradlew check` runs them on every build and a moved hash FAILS it** rather than printing a note. 4.3 added `rasterPinsAreStanding()` to assert, in one place, that the three are real values and mutually distinct - because a pin quietly reset to the all-zeros sentinel was previously visible only as three scattered "not pinned yet" notes. **Mutation-proved: resetting the flat-triangle pin to zeros fails both that pin's test and the new guard.**

**(4) Externally, by live sessions against the unmodified server.** Every step of Phase 4 was gated, and none of them are inferred:

| step | protocol | render |
|---|---|---|
| 4.1a | COMPATIBLE, 597/600, 98.4% | confirmed |
| 4.1c-1 | COMPATIBLE, 599/600, 96.7% | confirmed |
| 4.1c-2b | COMPATIBLE, 599/600, **99.0%** | models confirmed |
| 4.1c-2c | COMPATIBLE, 599/600, 98.8% | ground + markers confirmed |
| 4.2a | COMPATIBLE, 599/600, 98.9% | frame + overlays + login control |
| 4.2b | COMPATIBLE, 598/600, **99.3%** | default branch confirmed |

⚠⚠ **THE GAP, STATED PLAINLY: there is NO hash over a fixed SCENE.** The three pins cover the substrate and the triangle primitives; none of them drives the scene path end to end (projection -> queue -> tile draw -> model dispatch). 4.1's own scope flagged this, and it is still true.

**Why it is not built, rather than overlooked:** `WorldController.method315`/`method316` read `client.tileMarkers` as part of tile picking, which loads the `client` class - the same reason 4.1c-2c's hook placement could not be asserted headlessly. A scene-level hash would need either a headless-safe `client` or a recorded replay, and neither exists.

**So 4.3's claim is this, and no more:** the seam is provably **inert** (by construction and by assertion), it defers to primitives that are **pinned and mutation-proved**, and its end-to-end behaviour was **verified live six times** on both axes. What is **not** proven is scene-level byte-identity, and that must not be claimed on the strength of this section.

✅ **THE NON-DEFAULT BRANCH IS NOW CLOSED TOO (2026-10-05).** `renderer=placeholder` was exercised live against the unmodified server - the one path no static gate could reach. It behaved exactly as designed and left the default path's guarantees intact:

- **Both decline notices fired, EXACTLY ONCE EACH** - so the `reportedPresent`/`reportedScene` dedup works, and there is no per-frame log storm.
- **They fired at the RIGHT PHASES.** "presenting in software" during startup; "scene is drawn in software" only AFTER the map loads. ⚠ **That ordering is itself the evidence that the scene seam is genuinely reached** - a notice that had never appeared would have meant the seam was skipped.
- **Rendered IDENTICAL to software**, confirmed by the user. The placeholder is invisible in effect, which is the entire point of a declining renderer.
- **Protocol unmoved: COMPATIBLE, 599/600, LCS 97.4%, exit 0.** ⚠ **Worth stating: the renderer setting PROVABLY cannot affect packet framing** - the axis moved within its normal band (96.7-99.3%) purely because the route differed. That is separation of concerns demonstrated, not asserted.

⚠⚠ **The read path was proven in a closed loop rather than assumed, and the proof is on disk.** `ClientSettings` writes back `RendererConfig.requestedName()`, so had `select()` failed to read the key, or met an unrecognised value, it would have fallen back to `software` and **silently reverted the setting on exit**. The file's header comment was restamped `22:10:20` (it read `22:02:35` before), proving the client rewrote it; the key still read `placeholder`; therefore `requestedName()` was `placeholder`; therefore **`select()` read the key**. Restored to `renderer=software` afterwards via a single-key edit, so settings the session legitimately changed were preserved.

⚠ **Still open, and now the ONLY item Phase 4 carries forward:** the scene-level hash gap described above, and the remaining rasteriser entry points (`method319`/`method321`/`method305-309`/`method323-324`, `Model`'s `method472`/`method479`/`method483`/`method485`/`method480-486`), which stay deliberately unseamed. **No GL code is written anywhere in Phase 4.**

### Phase 5 — Model geometry export (additive, for the GPU path)
- [x] **5.1 Add read-only accessors** to `Model` for its geometry: vertex x/y/z, per-face colours, indices, and texture coordinates where present. ⚠️ **`Model`'s software rasteriser output must not change** — this phase only *exposes* what is already computed. ✅ **DONE 2026-10-05 — but NOT as literally worded: "post-transform geometry" could not mean screen-space, because the seam runs BEFORE the transform. See *Phase 5.1 DONE* below.**
- [x] **5.2 Add the buffer helpers** the GPU path will need (the RuneLite pattern: `GpuFloatBuffer`/`GpuIntBuffer` equivalents) — pure data holders, no GL calls yet. ✅ **DONE 2026-10-05 — two new files, no existing source touched. See *Phase 5.2 DONE* below.**
- [ ] **5.3 Verify with the Phase 0.4 harness** that exported geometry matches what the software rasteriser draws for the same model (a numeric cross-check, not a visual one).

#### Phase 5.1 DONE (2026-10-05) - and the wording it corrects

⚠⚠ **"POST-TRANSFORM" WAS WRONG AS WRITTEN, AND IT HAD TO BE SETTLED BEFORE ANY CODE.** 5.1 asked for "post-transform geometry". The natural reading is the screen-space scratch arrays `anIntArray1665/1666/1667` (screen x/y/depth) and `1668/1669/1670` (model-space coords used for texture mapping). **That reading is impossible at this seam:** `SceneRasterizer.dispatch` sits at the TOP of `method443` (`Model.java:2337`), and those arrays are only written *inside* the body, after the transform. At the moment a renderer is invoked they are still empty. ⚠ **They are also `static` scratch, shared by every model and valid only mid-draw** — so even if the seam were moved, they would be a poor export: invalidated by the next model drawn.

✅ **Resolved as: "post-transform" means post the model's OWN construction transform** — the model-local vertices, which is what a renderer should upload and transform itself. **This is the RuneLite arrangement 6.3 describes** ("confirm the CPU-side transform is the one uploaded"), and it is why 5.1 correctly precedes any GL.

**The field map, read off the real draw path (`method484`), not inferred:**

| field | meaning |
|---|---|
| `anInt1626` / `anInt1630` / `anInt1642` | vertex count / face count / **texture count** |
| `anIntArray1627/1628/1629` | model-local vertex x/y/z |
| `anIntArray1631/1632/1633` | face → vertex indices |
| `anIntArray1634/1635/1636` | per-corner LIT colours (written by the lighting pass) |
| `anIntArray1640` | face colour — ⚠️ **but the TEXTURE ID for a textured face**; see 5.3 |
| `anIntArray1639` | face alpha (0–255; the `(256+x)` normalisation is what identifies it) |
| `anIntArray1638` | face draw priority |
| `anIntArray1637` | low 2 bits = render type (0 flat-gouraud, 1 flat-single, 2 tex-gouraud, 3 tex-flat); `>> 2` = **texture-COORDINATE index**, ⚠️ **not the texture id** (corrected by 5.3) |
| `anIntArray1643/1644/1645` | texture-coordinate vertex indices, **indexed by the TEXTURE-COORDINATE index, not by face id** |

**What was added:** read-only accessors on `Model` itself — **+173 lines, 0 deletions, no existing line touched.** They had to live on `Model` rather than in a companion class because **nine of the arrays are `private`**, and Java `private` is class-scoped, so a same-package helper could not read them.

⚠ **The contract is explicit, because two of its edges are easy to get wrong:** the array accessors return the model's **LIVE storage, never a copy** (a copy per model per frame is the exact allocation the GPU path exists to avoid), so callers must not mutate them; and absent attributes return **`null`, never an invented sentinel** — `faceTextureId` returns **-1** for a face that is not textured, because **0 is a real texture**. The software path already guards this way (`if (anIntArray1639 == null)` in `method484`), so the accessors mirror it rather than diverging.

⚠ **CORRECTED BY 5.3 (2026-10-05): the texture-id half of the above was WRONG when 5.1 shipped.** 5.1 decoded the id as `anIntArray1637 >> 2`, but that is the texture-***coordinate*** index; the id the rasteriser samples is stored in **`anIntArray1640`** (the face *colour* slot) for textured faces. The accessor is now split into `faceTextureIndex` (the `>> 2` value) and `faceTextureId` (the colour slot). **No behaviour changed — nothing calls these yet — but the contract as documented would have made a GPU renderer sample the wrong texture.** See 5.3 for the evidence.

**Static gates:** harness **79 → 94/94** (15 new), `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly **100**. ⚠ **Mutation-proved on the mapping itself, which is the failure mode that matters — an export that reads the wrong field is silently wrong, not broken:** swapping `vertexYs` onto `anIntArray1627` fails the X/Y/Z-order test; making `faceTextureId` return 0 instead of -1 fails the absent-attribute test; making `textureCount` return the FACE count fails the texture-vs-face test. **Each mutation fails exactly its own assertion.** ✅ **LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05)** against the unmodified server: **COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 98.9%, exit 0**, and the user confirmed it rendered exactly as before.

⚠ **The strongest form of the additivity claim, checked mechanically rather than argued: THE NEW CODE HAS ZERO CALL SITES.** A search for every accessor name across `src/` returns only its own definition and comments in `Model.java` - nothing in the running client references any of them. ⚠ **So the addition is not merely inert by convention, it is unreachable from the live path** - which is *why* the protocol axis stayed in its usual band and the render axis could not have moved. That is a stronger statement than "we tested it and it looked fine", and it is the one to reach for if this seam is ever questioned.

⚠ **Deliberately not over-claimed: this step adds accessors and proves they read the right fields. It does NOT yet prove exported geometry matches what the rasteriser draws — that is 5.3's job.**

#### Phase 5.2 DONE (2026-10-05) - the two buffers, and the guard against them drifting

**What was added:** `ui/GpuFloatBuffer.java` and `ui/GpuIntBuffer.java` - growable primitive buffers with `put` overloads, a bulk `System.arraycopy` path, `ensureCapacity`, `position`/`capacity`/`get`/`clear`, and `putTriangle`. **Two NEW files with zero call sites; no existing source file was touched.**

⚠ **Why two classes rather than one generic buffer.** Java arrays are not covariant over primitives, so a single type would either **box every value - the exact allocation this path exists to avoid** - or store an `Object[]` and cast on every access.

⚠⚠ **And the cost of that choice is a divergence risk, which is the part worth calling out: two near-identical classes with no shared base will drift, and nothing in the language stops them.** So they carry the same method set with the same semantics, **and the harness runs an identical write sequence through both and asserts they report identical positions AND capacities.**

⚠ **The property the buffers exist for, and how it is pinned: after a warm-up a steady frame must REUSE its backing array.** The test asserts this by **array identity, not capacity** - a capacity that merely happens to match would still hide a reallocation. `clear()` resets the write position and keeps the storage, which is what makes the steady state allocation-free; **emptying must not mean freeing.**

**Contract, matching 5.1's:** `array()` returns **live storage, never a copy**; only the first `position()` entries are valid. There is **no limit and no flip** - a write is always an append past `position()`, which is the whole of the buffer's state, and a deliberately smaller surface than a real `java.nio.Buffer`. `get()` **fails fast** past `position()`: a buffer that returns stale data is the classic silent GPU corruption, because the upload reads garbage and nothing throws.

**Static gates:** harness **94 -> 118/118** (24 new), `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly **100**.

**Mutation-proved on four behaviours, each failing exactly its own assertion:**

| mutation | caught by |
|---|---|
| `clear()` reallocates instead of reusing | the array-identity reuse test (and the cross-buffer one) |
| growth via `new float[]` instead of `Arrays.copyOf` | "growth preserves the entries already written" |
| `get()` loses its bounds check | "fails fast instead of returning stale data" |
| **`GpuIntBuffer` growth changed to `+1` (only that class)** | **the symmetry test - i.e. the divergence guard is NOT vacuous** |

✅ **LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05)** against the unmodified server: **COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 99.3%, exit 0**, and the user confirmed it rendered exactly as before.

⚠ **Same unreachability check as 5.1, and the same result: zero call sites in `src/`.** Two new files referenced by nothing in the running client - which is *why* the protocol stayed in its usual band and the render axis could not have moved.

⚠ **Still no GL code, as 5.2 requires.** These are data holders; the upload representation (direct buffer vs heap array) is a Phase 7 decision and deliberately not pre-empted here. ⚠ **Also not claimed: that these buffers, or 5.1's accessors, produce geometry matching the software rasteriser - that is 5.3, and it is the step that would catch a wrong-field mapping in production rather than in a synthetic test.**

#### Phase 5.3 DONE (2026-10-05) - and it caught a WRONG CONTRACT in 5.1

**What it was for.** 5.1's tests wrote the private arrays directly with reflection, which proves the accessors read *those fields* but **cannot prove those fields are the right ones** — a self-consistent swap passes every test 5.1 had. 5.3 exists to close that gap by making the **parser** decide where each value lives.

**The method: craft a real-format model and parse it through the client's own loader.** `Model.method459` (initialise the store) → `method460` (parse) → the private `Model(int)` — the same three steps the client runs. The fixture is built in the genuine old-format layout, derived from `method460`'s own offset arithmetic rather than guessed:

```
0..2   vertex flags      (bit0=x, bit1=y, bit2=z delta)
3..4   face index types  (1 = three explicit deltas)
5..6   face render types (0 = flat, 2 = textured-gouraud)
7..12  face index deltas (Stream.method421 encoded, cumulative)
13..16 face colour slots (flat colour, then a TEXTURE ID)
17..22 texture coordinates (3 words: vertex indices 0,1,2)
23..34 x/y/z delta streams / 35..52 the 18-byte footer
```

`method421`'s two encodings are both exercised on purpose (a byte < 128 reads back as `value - 64`; otherwise `word - 49152`), so the z deltas of 100 use the two-byte form.

⚠⚠ **THE FINDING, and it is the reason this step was worth doing: 5.1's texture-id semantics were WRONG.** 5.1 decoded the texture id as `anIntArray1637 >> 2`. Driving the real rasteriser made it fail immediately:

```
Texture.method371(63488)  <- 63488 = 0xF800, the colour the fixture encoded
  at Texture.method378(1178)      <- k4 -> method371(k4)
  at Model.method484(2678)        <- last arg is anIntArray1640[i]
```

**`method378`'s last parameter is the id of the texture to SAMPLE, and `method484` fills it from `anIntArray1640` — the face *colour* slot.** Confirmed three ways, not one: (a) **all ten** `method378` call sites pass `anIntArray1640[i]` there; (b) `method481` explicitly **skips shading** a textured face (`if ((renderType & 2) == 2) return lightLevel;`), which only makes sense if the slot is not a colour; (c) the parser reads it from the colour stream and the alpha loader fills `anIntArray1639` separately. So **`anIntArray1640` is a colour for flat faces and a TEXTURE ID for textured faces** — the old-format convention — and `1637 >> 2` is the **texture-COORDINATE index** (which entry of `anIntArray1643/1644/1645`), not the id.

⚠ **Nothing rendered differently, because nothing calls these accessors yet — but a GPU renderer built on the 5.1 contract would have sampled the WRONG TEXTURE on every textured face**, and worse, the parse-and-inspect tests would have kept agreeing with it, since the export and the parser agreed with *each other*. **Only driving the real draw disputes it.** The accessors are now split: `faceTextureIndex(face)` (`word >> 2`) and `faceTextureId(face)` (the colour slot, `-1` when the face is not textured).

**The four checks, and what each can actually catch** (⚠ the plan's literal wording — "exported geometry matches what the rasteriser draws" — describes a comparison that **is not constructible until Phase 7**, because the export is not an input to the software rasteriser; that limitation is recorded here rather than papered over):

| check | catches |
|---|---|
| exact values through the REAL parser (positions, topology, texture coords, counts) | **wrong-field mapping** — the parser decides x/y/z, so a swap returns permuted values |
| every face index `< vertexCount()` | the rasteriser indexes `1665/1666` **unchecked**, so this is the property its safety rests on |
| decode agrees with `method484`'s own expressions; ids resolve | decode drift between export and rasteriser (⚠ a **drift guard**, not independent evidence — the expressions are the same) |
| **drive `method484` on the parsed model for BOTH faces** | **the whole class of "the export and the parser agree but are both wrong" errors — which is exactly what it found** |

The fixture carries **two faces on purpose**, one flat and one textured: a single-face fixture of either kind cannot distinguish the two colour-slot conventions, which is how the mistake survived 5.1. Topology is delta-coded and **cumulative**, so the second face decodes to 2,1,0 from deltas `(0,-1,-1)` — exercising the cumulative decode rather than repeating one triangle.

**Static gates:** harness **118 → 145/145** (27 new), `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly **100**. ⚠ **Mutation-proved, including a straight revert to the original bug:** restoring `faceTextureId = anIntArray1637 >> 2` fails **4** assertions; reading the id from the wrong array fails; and dropping the textured-face guard (so flat faces get an id) fails 3 — **the original mistake is now impossible to reintroduce silently.**

✅ **LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05)** against the unmodified server: **COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 98.4%, exit 0**, and the user confirmed it rendered exactly as before. ⚠ **This run loaded FOUR more map pairs than the previous one (262-269 in addition to 110-115 and 622-627), so it covered more ground than 5.2's session.** ⚠ **The render confirmation matters more here than for 5.1/5.2, because this step touched TEXTURE-ID semantics: if anything had read the changed accessor, textured faces would have drawn the wrong texture. Nothing did — the zero-call-site check above is what makes that prediction, and the live run is what confirms it.**

⚠ **What is still NOT claimed: that exported geometry RENDERS identically to the software path.** That needs a GPU renderer to feed the export to, i.e. Phase 7. Claiming it now would be unfalsifiable.

### Phase 6 — Animation preservation (474 frames/skins)
- [x] **6.1 Document the actual pipeline before touching it:** `Class36`/`Frames` (frames/skins) → `Skin` (skin transforms) → `Animable.method443` → `Model.method443`/`method478`. ✅ **DONE 2026-10-05 — see the 6.1 section below for the flow, the frame-key layout, the four opcodes, and the three writers with their MEASURED id ranges. The 28000+ invariant is now enforced by 14 harness assertions and mutation-proved four ways, so it no longer has "no test and nothing enforcing it".** ⚠️ This is the "474 skeletal structures and transform sequences" the brief refers to. ⚠️ **Updated 2026-10-04: the frame source is NOT just 474 data — there are three writers into one shared slot space**, and documenting *that* is the first thing this item has to do (see "the contract was missing a whole layer" above): (a) packed `Skins.dat`/`Frames.dat` (474), (b) **loose numeric `{id}.dat` in the cache root with id ≥ 100, auto-loaded and read by `load_647`** (e.g. Nex's `3502.dat`), and (c) the **`CursePack` remap into slots 28000+**. ⚠️ **The 28000+ base exists precisely so (c) cannot overwrite 474 frames that share the same 667 file ids** — that is a load-bearing invariant with no test and nothing enforcing it, and it is the single most likely thing a "cleanup" could silently break.
- [x] **6.2 Pin the transform contract with tests** (Phase 0.4 harness): for a set of frames, assert the post-transform vertex positions are unchanged. ⚠️ This is the safety net that makes the GPU path safe later — it must exist *before* any GL code. ✅ **DONE 2026-10-05 — exact vertices pinned for translate (`27,38,49`) and scale (`13,21,29`) through a real-format frame parsed against a real-format model carrying a SKIN stream; `method470`'s in-place compounding and `method464`'s copy (the actual drift guard) are both pinned. Harness 159 → 168; mutation-proved five ways after the first version was caught NOT testing the origin's `+ delta` term.**
- [x] **6.3 Confirm the CPU-side transform is the one uploaded** (RuneLite's approach), so GPU rendering cannot diverge from the software animation. ⚠️ **Do not move skinning into a shader** — that is where "animations stay fluid" turns into "animations drift". ✅ **DONE 2026-10-05 — pinned by test: the Phase 4 scene seam is handed the POSED model (`27,38,49`), not the rest pose (`20,30,40`), and it is the SAME object with LIVE storage, so there is no copy to go stale. The reason is the ordering — `applyAnimationFrame` runs in the BUILD step (`Player.getRotatedModel`), `method443` dispatches `this` at the DRAW step. Harness 168 → 174; mutation-proved three ways.**

#### Phase 6.1 DONE (2026-10-05) — the pipeline as it ACTUALLY runs

**The flow, each stage read off the code:**

```
Animation.anims[animId].anIntArray353[frameIdx]        def/Animation.java
  = PACKED FRAME KEY  (file << 16) | frame             <- a key, NOT an index
        |
Entity.frameId(anim, frame)                            game/Entity.java:206
  bounds-checks and returns -1 on ANY miss
        |
Model.method470(key)                              single frame
Model.applyAnimationFrame(key, next, end, cycle)  tween (client.tweeningEnabled)
        |
Frames.method531(key)   <- THE SLOT RESOLVER, the only place the store is read
  file = key >>> 16 ; frame = key & 0xffff ; return animationlist[file][frame]
        |
Frames (one per frame)                                 model/Frames.java:302
  aClass18_637      the Skin (skeleton) the file shares
  anInt638          how many labels this frame moves
  anIntArray639[k]  LABEL index -> Skin.anIntArray342 / anIntArrayArray343
  anIntArray640/641/642[k]   dx, dy, dz for that label
        |
Model.method472(opcode, labelIds, dx, dy, dz)      per label; writes VERTICES
  Skin.anIntArray342[label]           opcode 0..3
  Skin.anIntArrayArray343[label]      vertex LABEL IDs affected
  Model.anIntArrayArray1657[labelId]  the actual VERTEX indices carrying that label
        |
anIntArray1627/1628/1629  <- model-local vertices, mutated IN PLACE
        |
method479 (light) -> method443 (project) -> method484 (rasterise)
```

**The frame key is the thing not to get wrong** — a 32-bit pair, not an index. Three real values, and all three resolve to files that genuinely exist on disk:

| key | file | frame | what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| `229507364` | 3502 | 292 | Nex — 3502 **is** a loose file in the cache root |
| `141557823` | 2160 | 63 | loose file, **and also present in the packed 474 data** |
| `116457534` | 1777 | 62 | same |

**The four opcodes — and only 0..3 exist at all** (verified in `method472`):

| opcode | effect |
|---|---|
| 0 | moves the model ORIGIN (`anInt1681/2/3`) to the MEAN of the label's vertices, plus (dx,dy,dz). Sets the pivot the others use. |
| 1 | translates the label's VERTICES directly; leaves the origin alone. |
| 2 | rotates the label's vertices about the current origin, per-axis angles `(dx & 0xff) * 8` through the `modelIntArray1/2` sin/cos tables. |
| 3 | scales the label's vertices about the origin by (dx,dy,dz) / 128. |
| 4+ | **silently a no-op — there is no branch for them.** |

**THREE WRITERS, ONE SLOT SPACE — with the REAL numbers.** All three write `animationlist[file]`, separated only by the id range each chooses:

| # | writer | entry point | source | ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | packed 474 | `Frames.load(file)` ← `loadPacked` | `Skins.dat` + `Frames.dat` | **0..3229, dense** (3,230 / 3,229 entries) |
| 2 | loose numeric | `Frames.load_647(file)` | `{file}.dat` in the cache ROOT, id ≥ 100 | **exactly 3 today: 1777, 2160, 3502** |
| 3 | CursePack 667 | `Frames.load(slot, data)` ← `CurseData667.loadAnimationFile` | `CursePack/anims/{srcId}.gz` | **28000..28006** (7 files) |

⚠️ **THE INVARIANT, NOW CONCRETE RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The CursePack's *source* file ids are `{2998, 3012, 3013, 3016, 3018, 3019, 3020}` — **all of them INSIDE the packed range 0..3229.** Loading them under their own ids would have **overwritten 474 frame files**. `ANIM_FILE_BASE = 28000` moves them clear (28000 > 3229, with room to spare), and `CurseData667.remapFile` rewrites each `anIntArray353` entry to `(ANIM_FILE_BASE + i) << 16 | index`, so the client asks for slot 28000+ and the 474 file is never touched. **That is the load-bearing reason the base exists — and until now nothing enforced it.**

⚠️ **DISPATCH PRECEDENCE, which is not obvious.** `method531` tries a writer **only when the slot is EMPTY**, so whichever runs first wins and the rest are skipped: **(1)** loose `{file}.dat` if it exists, **(2)** packed, **(3)** CursePack (only reachable for file ≥ 28000), **(4)** on-demand fetch, which returns null for this frame. At startup it is **eager then lazy**: `loadFrames()` runs `loadPacked` and **then** `loadLooseFrameFiles()`, which walks the cache root and pre-loads every numeric `{id}.dat` ≥ 100.

⚠️⚠️ **THE FINDING THAT FALLS OUT OF THAT — and it is the thing a cleanup would break: 1777 AND 2160 ALSO EXIST IN THE PACKED 474 DATA.** Measured by parsing both archives: `Frames.dat` holds ids 0..3229, `Skins.dat` holds 0..3228, and **both contain 1777 and 2160; neither contains 3502**, so Nex is purely additive. For those two ids the loose file therefore **silently REPLACES the 474 packed frames** rather than adding to them — because the loose load runs after `loadPacked` and `method531` never re-reads a populated slot. **Reordering those two startup lines, or deleting either loose file, would quietly swap those animations back to their 474 versions with nothing failing.** ⚠ **Whether the override is intended is NOT asserted here** — it is recorded as behaviour to confirm before Phase 6.5 builds on it.

**Other hazards worth writing down before anything is touched:**
- `loadLooseFrameFiles` accepts **any** numeric `{id}.dat` ≥ 100 in the cache root and feeds it to the frame decoder. Today that is exactly 3 files and all are frame archives — the real cache (`main_file_cache.dat`) is safe **only because its basename is not all digits**.
- `load_647` and `method531` both swallow exceptions (`catch (Exception) {}`), so a malformed injected file yields **no animation, not an error** — a bad 667 injection therefore looks like a content bug rather than a load bug.
- `method472` has **no branch for opcode 4+**, so an unexpected opcode is a silent no-op, not a crash. The injector's opcode expectations cannot be validated by the rasteriser.
- `method531` returns null for a frame index past the file's own length and `Entity.frameId` returns -1 for a miss, so a partially-loaded animation degrades **frame-by-frame** rather than failing whole — which is why a **wrong** slot is far more noticeable than a missing one.

**What this step does NOT do:** it changes no production code, and it deliberately does not pin the *transform* (that is 6.2). ⚠ **But its central claim IS now enforced rather than merely written: the slot-budget invariant — the thing the plan calls "the single most likely thing a cleanup could silently break" — has four new harness groups and no longer has "no test and nothing enforcing it".**

**The invariant, pinned (harness 145 → 159/159, 14 new):**

| assertion | why it is the one that matters |
|---|---|
| `remapSequenceFrames` moves 2998 → slot 28000 and 3020 → 28006, **preserving the frame index** | pins the remap arithmetic itself |
| a **non-curse** key (1777, a real loose file) is left **exactly** as it was; -1 and 0 are untouched | pins *"Never steal OG frame slots"* — without this the remap could quietly become a catch-all |
| `loadAnimationFile` **refuses** 1777, 27999, 28007 and negatives | pins that the window cannot widen onto the original ids (asserted only out-of-range, so the test does not depend on a CursePack being installed) |
| `ANIM_FILE_BASE == 28000`, 7 source files, **max source 3020 < base** | pins the *separation* of the two ranges, not one constant in isolation |

⚠⚠ **Mutation-proved four ways, each failing:** setting the base to 3000 (**which is inside the packed 0..3229 range — i.e. the exact collision the base exists to prevent**) fails; making `remapFile` a catch-all fails the OG-slot test; shifting the window by one fails; dropping the frame index during the remap fails. ⚠ **One honest limit: the `frame <= 0` guard in `remapSequenceFrames` is belt-and-braces and NOT independently pinned** — `remapFile` already leaves -1 and 0 alone (`-1 >>> 16 == 65535`, which is not a curse id), so removing that guard would not change the observable behaviour the tests assert. What is pinned is the behaviour; the guard is an optimisation.

#### Phase 6.2 DONE (2026-10-05) — the transform contract, pinned

**What is now pinned, exactly:** a frame parsed from the REAL frame format, applied to a model parsed from the REAL model format, produces **specific vertex positions** — not "does not throw".

| assertion | the number |
|---|---|
| a translate frame moves the label-6 vertex by exactly the encoded delta and **leaves the label-5 vertices untouched** | `(20,30,40) + (7,8,9) = (27,38,49)`; v0 `(0,0,0)` and v1 `(10,20,30)` unchanged |
| opcode 0 sets the origin to the **mean of the label's vertices PLUS the frame delta** | mean `(5,10,15)` + delta `(1,2,3)` = `(6,12,18)` |
| a **scale** frame scales about that origin by `(j,k,l)/128` | `(20,30,40) → (13,21,29)` |
| transforms are deterministic across independent models | identical on both |

**The fixtures are in the real formats, and that is the point.** The model fixture is 5.3's real-format parse **extended with a SKIN stream** — one label per vertex, with the footer's 5th flag (`k1`) set so `anInt376` points at it. ⚠ **The skin stream is the whole reason the transform can run at all**: `method470` returns immediately when `anIntArrayArray1657` is null, and that map is only built by `method469()` **from per-vertex labels**, so the labels have to come through the parser. The frame fixture is the real `Frames.decodeFrames` layout (Skin → frame count → frame index → group count → one flag byte per label → 2-byte deltas where the flag bits are set). **Hand-setting the arrays instead would have proved the transform reads the fields the test wrote; parsing them proves the fields are the right ones** — the distinction 5.3 was built to make.

⚠⚠ **THE FINDING, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS FOR PHASE 7: `method470` mutates `anIntArray1627/1628/1629` IN PLACE, so it is NOT idempotent — applying the same frame twice compounds** (`27,38,49 → 34,46,58`, asserted). **What stops animation drifting frame over frame is `method464`** (`Player.java:308`): the client transforms a shared scratch model (`Model.aModel_1621`) after `method464` has **COPIED** the base vertices into it. That copy is now pinned from both sides — the source model must be untouched, and the copy must be the one that moved. ⚠ **Mutating `method464` to alias the source arrays instead of copying fails that test immediately, which is precisely the "animations stay fluid → animations drift" failure this phase exists to prevent.**

**Static gates:** harness **159 → 168/168** (9 new), `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly **100**. ⚠ **Mutation-proved five ways, all caught:** aliasing in `method464` instead of copying; opcode 1 subtracting instead of adding; opcode 3 dividing by 64 instead of 128; opcode 0 dropping the encoded delta; opcode 0 dividing the mean by `j1 + 1`.

⚠⚠ **AND THE MUTATION RUN CAUGHT A HOLE IN MY OWN TEST, which is recorded here rather than quietly fixed: the first version of the origin assertion did NOT pin the `+ delta` term.** The fixture used a **zero** delta for label 5, so removing `+ j` from `anInt1681 = anInt1681 / j1 + j` changed **nothing** and the mutation was **MISSED**. The fixture now encodes a deliberately non-zero delta `(1,2,3)`, which is why the expected origin is `(6,12,18)` and not `(5,10,15)` — **the assertion only tests what the fixture actually exercises.** ⚠ **A second setup failure in the same run (a wrong indentation in the mutation's search string) is also recorded: a mutation that never runs is not evidence, and "CAUGHT: 4 of 4" would have been a false claim.**

**What this step does NOT do:** it changes no production code (no live gate needed — see below), and it deliberately does **not** touch the GPU path. It pins the *CPU-side* transform; **6.3 then confirms that this same CPU transform is the one uploaded**, because the plan forbids moving skinning into a shader.

#### Phase 6.3 DONE (2026-10-05) — the seam receives the CPU-transformed geometry

**The question 6.3 asks, answered by test rather than by reading:** when a rasteriser is installed, does it get the **posed** vertices or the **rest pose**? The answer is the posed ones — and the ordering that makes it so is not visible from either method alone:

```
Player.getRotatedModel()          <- the BUILD step
  model_2.method464(model_1, ...)   copies BASE geometry into the scratch
  model_2.applyAnimationFrame(...)  <- method470 runs HERE, mutating anIntArray1627/8/9
  return model_2
        |
        v   ... later, the DRAW step
Model.method443()                 <- dispatches `this` FIRST, before any early return
  SceneRasterizer.dispatch(this, ...)   <- the model handed over ALREADY holds posed vertices
```

⚠ **So skinning never needs to move into a shader — not as a policy preference, but because the geometry arriving at the seam is already transformed.** That is the RuneLite shape the plan asks for, and it is now pinned.

| assertion | why it is the one that matters |
|---|---|
| the seam is **actually reached** by `method443` | without it, every other assertion in this test is vacuous |
| control: an **unanimated** model dispatched through the SAME seam reads the **rest pose** `(20,30,40)` | proves the difference below is the animation, not the fixture |
| the posed model reads `(27,38,49)` — **not** the rest pose | the core 6.3 claim: the CPU transform is already folded in |
| the seam is handed **the same object** that was posed (`rec.lastModel == posed`) | there is no intermediate copy that could go stale |
| `vertexXs()` **is the array `method472` wrote** (identity, not equality) | a rasteriser needs no shader skinning, because it reads the live storage |

⚠ **The last two are the interesting ones.** A "rest-pose-versus-rest-pose" comparison would pass while being completely wrong, which is why the control is dispatched through the *same* seam rather than compared against a constant, and why the animated-versus-rest difference is asserted explicitly. And the **identity** check (`==`, not `equals`) is what rules out a defensive copy — a renderer holding a snapshot of the vertices would silently stop animating.

**Static gates:** harness **168 → 174/174** (6 new), `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly **100**. ⚠ **Mutation-proved three ways, all caught:** removing the seam call (fails "actually reached"); making opcode 1 transform a **temp** so the model stays at rest pose (fails "sees the ANIMATED vertex"); making `vertexXs()` hand out a **clone** instead of live storage (fails the identity check).

⚠⚠ **Honest limit: "the renderer is handed the WRONG OBJECT" is guarded by the identity assertion but is NOT mutation-proved.** Expressing that mutation means dispatching a placeholder model inside `method443`, which would NPE or corrupt other tests rather than fail cleanly — so the guard exists and is asserted, but it has not been shown to *fail* under mutation the way the other three have. **Recorded rather than claimed.**

**What this step does NOT do:** no production code changed (no live gate needed — only the harness differs, per `git diff --numstat`), and no GL code is written. It establishes the fact Phase 7 depends on: **the vertex arrays a GPU backend must upload are the ones `method472` has already written, and uploading them needs no shader-side skinning.**

### Phase 6.5 — A real 667 data pipeline (user-requested direction, added 2026-10-04)
⚠️ **This is feature work, not refactoring, and it is explicitly the user's stated future direction** ("i plan to load more 667 data whilst keeping the 474 data aswell"). It is placed here because **it must not be attempted before Phases 3 and 6**, for one reason: today the 667 injections are ad-hoc (hardcoded ids, magic ranges, hand-written opcode skippers), and building *more* on that foundation multiplies the collision risk above. The reference material is `C:\Users\llrbi\Desktop\667 Data` — a RuneJS-based toolchain holding full 667 definitions (`667npc/loc/obj/seq/spotanim/idk.dat` + `.idx`) and 667 model/anim folders, ~152 MB, most of which is `node_modules`.
- [x] **6.5.1 Decide the model before the mechanism.** ✅ **DECIDED 2026-10-05, BY THE USER: the EXPLICIT REGISTRY - not more magic ranges.** One place declaring (id, source revision, source file, target slot) that the loader consults. ⚠ **The precondition is met for the first time because Phase 6 documented and test-covered the existing paths** - and Phase 6 also converted this from a hypothetical risk into a measured one (the CursePack's 28000 offset and the 1777/2160 shadowing both exist to paper over the old convention). **`model.FrameSlots` is already the precedent: the frame-slot ranges moved out of three separate literals into one owner in 6.5.2.**
- [x] **6.5.2 Give the frame-slot budget a single owner.** ✅ **DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-10-05: `model.FrameSlots` owns the budget and all three writers consult it; harness 187/187; the live gate passed (COMPATIBLE, prefix 595/600, LCS 97.8%, CursePack 9/9). ⚠⚠ The one gap unit tests could not reach - a never-firing `isLooseSlot` - now has a repeatable probe that CATCHES that exact mutation (`tools/loose-frame-probe/run.ps1 -Mutate`). See the phase section below.**
- [x] **6.5.3 Reuse the 474 readers where the formats agree, and say so explicitly where they do not** (`SpotAnim.readValues667` is the existing precedent for "the format genuinely differs"). ⚠ **A 667 definition reader is an external format contract, exactly like a packet read** - it may be moved and tested, never "tidied". ⚠⚠ **DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-10-05 (harness 192/192; live gate COMPATIBLE at prefix 595/600 and LCS 98.6%).** ⚠ **`readValues667` turned out NOT to be that precedent at all - it is a pass-through visibility shim, now deleted; the formats genuinely agree on every opcode the real pack uses, and the one divergence (seq opcode 12) never occurs in it. See the section below.**
