Note the asymmetry: 667 content cannot break *server* compatibility (it never touches the wire), but it can break *474 content*, which is what the Phase 0.4 harness and the golden master exist to catch.

#### Phase 6.5.2 DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05) - the slot budget has one owner

**What changed:** `model.FrameSlots` is now the single owner of the frame-slot budget, and the three writers consult it instead of re-deriving their bounds locally.

| what | before | now |
|---|---|---|
| loose floor | `id >= 100` inside `loadLooseFrameFiles` | `FrameSlots.isLooseSlot(id)` |
| curse window | `ANIM_FILE_BASE = 28000` + `local < 0 || local >= 7` | `FrameSlots.isCurseSlot(id)` / `CURSE_BASE` |
| remap + preload | `ANIM_FILE_BASE + i` in two places | `FrameSlots.CURSE_BASE + i` |
| key limit | **not expressed anywhere** | `FrameSlots.MAX_FILE_ID = 32767` |

⚠ **This is a MOVE, not a change, and that was verified two ways: by hand, that each rewritten condition accepts/rejects exactly the same ids as the literal it replaced** (the old `local < 0 || local >= 7` and `!isCurseSlot(id)` reject precisely the same set), **and by test.** ⚠ **Nothing about precedence moved** - which writer wins a contested slot is still decided by `method531` (EQUAL-EMPTY) and by `loadFrames` running packed-then-loose; `FrameSlots` owns only the NUMBERS, and says so.

⚠ **`MAX_FILE_ID` is the one thing here that was previously INEXPRESSIBLE.** `Animation.anintArray353` packs the file into the HIGH 16 bits, so `file << 16` must stay a positive `int`; an id above 32767 silently yields a NEGATIVE key and the frame simply never resolves. ⚠ **The CursePack window (28000..28006) is inside the limit, but nothing stated the limit before - and the loose loader still accepts any `{id}.dat` >= 100 with no upper bound.** That is recorded as a hazard rather than silently changed, because rejecting high ids would change what loads.

⚠ **6.5.2's stated minimum bar is met: "a test that asserts the 474 slots still decode identically after an injection".** A CursePack injection at 28000 is performed, and a 474-range slot (500) must decode to `27,38,49` both before AND after - with a NON-VACUOUS control proving the two slots genuinely hold different data, so the comparison cannot pass by comparing nothing.

**Static gates:** harness **174 → 187/187** (13 new), `clean check installBin` green, warnings exactly **100**. ⚠ **Mutation-proved four ways, all caught:** moving `CURSE_BASE` to 3000 (inside the packed range - the exact collision it prevents); raising `LOOSE_MIN_ID` to 1000; raising `MAX_FILE_ID` to 65535; dropping `CURSE_COUNT` to 5.

⚠⚠ **AND ONE HONEST GAP, RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER: the fifth mutation - wiring `loadLooseFrameFiles` to a predicate that never fires - is NOT caught by any unit test.** It cannot be, because that method reads the real cache directory and no test supplies one. ⚠ **The live gate is what covers it:** the real cache root holds exactly 1777, 2160 and 3502, all of which load through that call, so a broken predicate would visibly break those animations. ⚠ **THAT WAS THE STATE AT IMPLEMENTATION TIME - THE PROBE BELOW NOW CLOSES IT, so this paragraph is history, not an open gap.**

⚠ **A CAPTURE WAS SUBMITTED FOR THIS GATE AND REJECTED - recorded here so it is never mistaken for the 6.5.2 evidence.** `packet-tap-20261005-223450.log` (4089 units, 4038 received / 51 sent, COMPATIBLE, prefix 599/600, LCS 98.4%) is a clean, valid capture **of the client as it stood BEFORE 6.5.2**, so it exercises the OLD code path and cannot close this phase. ⚠ **Proved by BYTECODE, not by the clock alone:** the committed `Proxy Client/bin/def/CurseData667.class` references `ANIM_FILE_BASE` and contains no `FrameSlots` symbol at all, and `bin/` was never cleaned (436 classes, most dated 09-20..10-01), so the class that tap ran against is the pre-6.5.2 one. The timestamps agree: the log was written at **22:35:33**, `src/model/FrameSlots.java` was created at **22:57:41**, the 6.5.2 sources last changed at **22:59:25**, and `bin/` + `build/` were rebuilt at **22:59:34-36**. ⚠ **What that capture IS good for:** it re-proves the PRE-EDIT baseline was COMPATIBLE at 599/600 and 98.4%, which is exactly the control the post-edit run should be judged against - it is just not the gate itself.

⚠ **Static gate re-run after the 6.5.2 edit and green: `passed: 187  failed: 0`, `clean check installBin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, warnings exactly 100.**

⚠⚠ **LIVE GATE PASSED (2026-10-05 23:06) ON A BUILD THAT PROVABLY CONTAINED THE CHANGE.** Capture `packet-tap-20261005-230541.log` was written at **23:06:19**, against `bin/` built at **22:59:36** - and decisively, the bytecode it loaded references `FrameSlots` and `isCurseSlot`/`isLooseSlot` with `ANIM_FILE_BASE` GONE, so the tap exercised the REWIRED predicates rather than the old literals. **All four of 6.5.2's stated checks pass:** **(a) protocol** - COMPATIBLE, prefix **595/600** (need >= 590), LCS **97.8%** (need >= 75%), exit 0, 4153 units (4095 received / 58 sent); no wire code was touched, and this is consistent with the previous baseline's 599/600 and 98.4%. **(b) loose frames still load** - proven by the probe below; this was the only check with no unit-test coverage. **(c) CursePack still injects at 28000+** - the startup log prints `CursePack models loaded: 9/9` and `Injected curse seq/gfx from CursePack (frames remapped)`, so the remap still resolves to the high slots. **(d) animations still play** - no exceptions and a normal `Client exited (code 0)` across the 667 content (walked, attacked, picked up items, past Nex / curse / Bloat and the Rock Crab area).

⚠⚠ **THE RECORDED GAP IS NOW CLOSED, NOT EYEBALLED.** `tools/loose-frame-probe/run.ps1` compiles the probe in `tools/loose-frame-probe/` - a directory gradle does NOT compile (`sourceSets.main.java.srcDirs = ['src']`), so it cannot reach the shipped client - and calls the REAL `Frames.loadFrames()` against the REAL `%USERPROFILE%\Biohazard.474` root, which holds exactly 1777.dat, 2160.dat and 3502.dat. Result: `animationlist` **1777 -> 546**, **2160 -> 1695**, **3502 -> 1236**. ⚠ **Two controls make that non-vacuous:** `frameData[500]` is present, proving the packed path genuinely decoded, and `animationlist[500]` is **0**, proving packed-only ids do NOT enter that array - so the three non-zero results can only come from the loose loader. ⚠ **And it is mutation-proved: `run.ps1 -Mutate` rebuilds `FrameSlots` with `isLooseSlot` returning false into a shadow directory, after which the probe reports `LOOSE_FAIL` (exit 1) with `animationlist` never allocated.** That is exactly the fifth mutation the harness misses, so the gap is no longer merely "left to the live gate" - it has a repeatable check that fails the moment the wiring breaks.

⚠ **Why the probe is shaped this way:** `loadPacked` fills `frameData` (a `byte[][]`) only, while `animationlist[file]` is populated ONLY by the loose loader during `loadFrames` (the lazy decode in the accessor runs on access, not at load). So a non-zero `animationlist[1777]` is specific evidence that `FrameSlots.isLooseSlot` fired, rather than a general sign that *something* loaded.

#### Phase 6.5.3 DONE AND LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-10-05) - reuse the 474 readers where the formats agree

**The correction comes first, because it reframes the phase.** This plan cited `SpotAnim.readValues667` as "the existing precedent that the format genuinely differs". It is not. It is:

```java
void readValues667(Stream stream) { readValues(stream); }
```

⚠ **A pure pass-through, and it existed for a VISIBILITY reason, not a format one:** `SpotAnim.readValues` was `private`, and `CurseData667` is in the same package and needed to call it. So the 667 "variant" was a package shim whose name asserted a difference that does not exist - the kind of thing that makes the next reader believe the formats differ and hand-roll a second parser. **The alias is now deleted and `readValues` is public**, so the reuse is literal and self-describing.

**The measurement that grounds "the formats agree"** (walk both real CursePack files with each path's opcode table, requiring the walk to land on exact EOF):

| file | entries | bytes | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `seq.dat` | 15371 | 2516296 | lands EXACTLY on EOF, **0** bad opcodes; opcodes used: 0,1,2,3,5,6,7,8,9,10,11 |
| `spotanim.dat` | 2982 | 29046 | lands EXACTLY on EOF, **0** bad opcodes; opcodes used: 0,1,2,4,5,6,7,8,40 |

⚠⚠ **The ONE genuine divergence, recorded rather than tidied: seq opcode 12.** The skip table consumes one byte for it; `Animation.readValues` has **no case for 12** and consumes nothing (it prints `Unrecognized seq.dat config code: 12`). That is a real difference in where the cursor lands - **but opcode 12 occurs ZERO times in the real pack**, so it is latent. Removing that case would change nothing today and would silently break the pack the day it uses 12, which is exactly why it is asserted instead of removed.

⚠ **Why the skip tables exist at all, stated explicitly rather than left as an accident:** the readers **allocate** per entry (opcode 1 builds three `int[]` of length n), so decoding the ~15k UNWANTED entries only to discard them would churn garbage. The hand-written walks are a deliberate optimisation - and their cost is a second copy of the opcode knowledge, which is the drift risk 6.5.3 has to bound.

**The drift guard (the durable part).** 5 new tests pin the skip tables against the readers **opcode by opcode**, and two design choices are what give them teeth:

⚠ **One continuous stream, not one stream per entry.** A misalignment at entry i then shifts every later boundary, so a one-byte disagreement cannot hide behind a per-entry coincidence.
⚠ **Poison (non-opcode) payload bytes.** This is the part I got WRONG first: with small payload values, a mutated skipper that consumed one byte where the reader consumes two landed on another VALID opcode, coincidentally re-synced, and the mutation **PASSED** - a drift guard that could not detect drift. With poison payloads the same mutation hits an unrecognised opcode and stops the walk early, and the test fails with `opcode 7 skip=35 read=36`. **The comment in the fixture records this, because the lesson matters more than a tidy-looking test.**

⚠ **Mutation-proved: seq opcode 7 changed from a word to a byte -> FAIL (2 failures, `skip=35 read=36`), build FAILED. Reverted and re-gated green.**

**Static gates:** harness 187 -> **192/192** (5 new), `clean check installBin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, warnings exactly **100**.

⚠⚠ **LIVE GATE PASSED (2026-10-05 23:22) ON A BUILD THAT PROVABLY CONTAINED THE CHANGE - and the bytecode proof here doubles as a warning about `bin/`.** Capture `packet-tap-20261005-232207.log` was written at **23:22:32** against `bin/def/` built at **23:20:13**, and `javap` on the classes it actually loaded confirms the rewiring: `def.SpotAnim` declares **`public void readValues(net.Stream)`** and contains **no `readValues667` at all**, while `def.CurseData667` issues **`invokevirtual def/SpotAnim.readValues:(Lnet/Stream;)V`** - the 667 spotanim path is the 474 reader by name, exactly as intended. **(a)** protocol COMPATIBLE, prefix **595/600** (need >= 590), LCS **3911/3965 = 98.6%** (need >= 75%), 3965 units (3929 received / 36 sent), exit 0. **(b)** the walk still lands on the correct boundaries: the startup log shows `CursePack: C:\Users\llrbi\Biohazard.474\CursePack`, `CursePack models loaded: 9/9` and `Injected curse seq/gfx from CursePack (frames remapped)` - which for a file walk is the real evidence, since a desync corrupts the injected definitions rather than necessarily throwing. **(c)** 667 content visited as the capture script asks.

⚠⚠ **THE `bin/` GOTCHA, PRE-EXISTING RATHER THAN CAUSED HERE.** `bin/` still holds **212 default-package `.class` files from BEFORE the Phase 3.1 package move**, including **`bin/SpotAnim.class` and `bin/CurseData667.class` (both 2026-10-04 23:14) which STILL contain `readValues667`**. A naive "does the build still contain readValues667?" scan over `bin/` therefore returns a **FALSE POSITIVE** and looks like the change never landed. ⚠ **The capture is NOT affected: nothing in the current build references those copies.** `javap` over every packaged class finds **zero** bare references to them, and the startup log prints the CursePack sequence **exactly once** (from `def.CurseData667`), so Java resolves `def.*` by name and the stale copies are unreachable. ⚠ They are **unmodified vs HEAD**, so no phase introduced them, and `reportStaleClasses` already lists them under **"(Phase 8 cleanup)"** - known and scheduled, not a silent trap. ⚠ **Recorded anyway, because the next person verifying a `def/`-level change by grepping `bin/` will hit exactly this.**

⚠ **The measurement is now REPEATABLE rather than a one-off: `tools/curse-opcode-probe/run.ps1`.** It runs BOTH shipping paths over the REAL pack (calling the pack's private skipper and the 474 reader by reflection, so it cannot drift from what it checks) and requires the cursor to land identically after every entry, and both walks to reach exact EOF. **On the real pack: seq 15371/15371 entries walked by both, both ending exactly at byte 2516296; spotanim 2982/2982, both ending exactly at 29046.** ⚠ **Agreement is checked PER ENTRY, so the walk is proven identical at every boundary, not merely at the end.**

⚠⚠ **That agreement is what PROVES the opcode-12 divergence is latent, and it is a stronger result than the histogram.** A single opcode 12 makes the reader consume nothing where the skipper consumes one byte, so the cursor ends up one byte out and every later boundary disagrees - and there is no path that re-syncs them, because a skipper only ever stops early or consumes a fixed amount. So "no entry disagrees" is only possible if 12 never occurs. ⚠ **The probe reports entry-start opcodes only (the first opcode of each entry) and labels them as such** - a FULL histogram is not observable from it, because `Stream` is `final` and the skippers do not report what they read - so the probe adds no third opcode table. Its entry-start counts cross-check the independent measurement: 15172 and 2844 entries begin with opcode 1, matching that histogram exactly.

⚠⚠ **The probe is mutation-proved too, and the failure CORRECTS something I had written here first.** Making the skipper read opcode 7 as a byte instead of a word fails it (exit 1) - but not by corrupting quietly: the desynchronised skipper ran PAST EOF and threw `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` out of `CurseData667.inject`, which has no `try`/`catch` of its own and is called during client startup (`client.java:8259`). **So a desync has two outcomes and BOTH are bad:** it throws if it overruns the file, and it silently mangles every later definition if the drift happens to stay inside it. That is exactly why the live gate cannot be replaced by "it did not crash". The probe reports either outcome as a verdict (offending entry, walk counts, first diverging offset) instead of escaping as a stack trace, because a check whose whole job is to be readable when something is wrong should not fail by crashing.

⚠ **"What is NOT covered" is now narrower but not empty, and is recorded rather than implied:** the hermetic guard can only pin the two tables against EACH OTHER, not against the real pack - the probe is what closes that - and the full opcode histogram remains a non-hermetic one-off. ⚠ Also still true and worth knowing: `CurseData667`'s `catch` blocks around gzip/model reads are empty, so those failures stay silent.

### Phase 7 — OpenGL backend (proceed only after 0–6, all verified) ⚠️ LAST
- [ ] **7.1 ⚠️ Do NOT use `Display.setParent`** — it deadlocks Windows here already. Use **LWJGL 3** with `org.lwjgl.opengl.awt.AWTGLCanvas` (single AWT component), or an offscreen context, and record the choice with the reason.
- [ ] **7.2 Implement the GL backend behind Phase 4's seam**, hybrid by design: 3D scene via GL, **UI/chat/tabs stay software and composite on top** (the RuneLite seam). This is what keeps the work bounded.
- [ ] **7.3 Keep the software path forever as a fallback**, selectable, and the default until the GL path has proven itself across a long session.
- [ ] **7.4 Verify the full checklist below on a live client**, plus the golden master, plus a long session for stability (context loss, resolution change, alt-tab focus).

### Phase 8 — Cleanup (last, after the GL path is settled)
- [ ] **8.1 Delete dead weight:** `dump4/`, `jdt-bin/`, and the LWJGL 2 jars if Phase 7 supersedes them.
- [ ] **8.2 Remove superseded code:** `LwjglPresent` (and `GlPresent` if Java2D present is replaced), and any `class`/`jar` no longer referenced. ⚠️ Verify by *absence of imports and a clean compile*, not by filename.
- [ ] **8.3 Reconcile `src` and `bin`** so a stale class file can never be loaded after a rename (`Compile.bat`'s `robocopy` does not delete removed classes — this is how a renamed class silently keeps working from a stale `.class`).
- [ ] **8.4 Final golden-master run against the unmodified server.**

## Verification checklist (the gate for every phase)

1. **Compiles** with the pinned toolchain, warning count not increased.
2. **Logs into the unmodified server** and reaches the game world.
3. **Golden-master route replays identically** (Phase 0.3) — position, animation, and no desync over several minutes.
4. **Combat, item pickup/drop, and a level-up** behave as recorded (these are where cache/model/anim bugs surface).
5. **Cache-heavy actions** work: region change, teleport, NPC spawn, animation-heavy scene (the Rock Crab area where the crash happened).
6. **No new crash** in the console, and specifically the Phase 2 hardening cases hold.
7. **`ObjectCollisionSizes` still matches** the server's `Data/objectSize.cfg`.
8. For Phase 7 only: **software fallback still produces an identical framebuffer**, and alt-tab/resolution-change does not deadlock or lose the GL context.

5. **OPEN QUESTION (raised 2026-10-05, user deferred): do the loose frame files `1777.dat` and `2160.dat` INTEND to override the packed 474 frames of the same file ids?** ⚠ Measured: `Frames.dat` holds ids 0..3229 and `Skins.dat` 0..3228, and **both contain 1777 and 2160**, while the cache root also holds them as loose files. Because `loadFrames()` runs `loadPacked` and THEN `loadLooseFrameFiles()`, and `method531` never re-reads a populated slot, **the loose file wins and the 474 content for those two ids is unreachable at runtime.** ⚠ **This is recorded as behaviour, NOT as intent** - deleting either loose file, or reordering those two startup lines, would silently revert those animations with nothing failing. Owned by 6.5.2.
5. **Java target decision is deferred to Phase 1** with a recommendation (8 → 17) rather than fixed now, because it should be landed against the golden master rather than chosen on paper.
6. **`bin/` is git-tracked** — every phase's rebuild shows up as a large binary diff. Decide whether that stays (it does make rollout simple) or moves to a build artifact; either way record it.
7. ✅ **RESOLVED — was: "the client's `HEAD` does not build."** Fixed by Phase 0.6 (`f3ec2e1`), and the whole working tree was then checkpointed by the user as **`69bd4d3 "before client refactor try"`** (13,815 files). ⚠️ **This materially improves the plan's safety position: there is now a real fallback commit**, so the ~46 previously-uncommitted client files and the repo-wide deletions are recoverable. Both `f3ec2e1` (the 7-class fix) and `69bd4d3` are in history. Keep this in mind for Phase 3: **the client now has a restore point, so `git clean`/`checkout` are no longer fatal — but they will still discard uncommitted work, so commit before each risky phase.**
8. **Stray `.bak-pre-orig` files sit in `src/`** (`Animation.java.bak-pre-orig`, `Class36.java.bak-pre-orig`) — leftovers from an earlier editing session. Harmless to `javac` (not `*.java`) but they are history and intent that should be either committed deliberately or removed deliberately, in Phase 8.

#### Phase 4.1c-2c SCOPED AND DONE (2026-10-05) - THREE decision points, not two, and not the same shape as 4.1c-2b

**The ground is drawn by TWO methods, not one.** Measured per method over `WorldController.java`:

| method | mesh it draws | rasteriser calls |
|---|---|---|
| `method315` (decl L1608) | `Class43` = `Ground.aClass43_1311` - the ground/floor mesh | **7** (`method374` x4, `method378` x3) |
| `method316` (decl L1899) | `Class40` = `aClass40_1312` - the per-tile overlay mesh | **4** (`method374` x2, `method378` x2) |

11 in total, which is where the earlier figure came from - but it is **split across two methods**, and `method316` alone has only 4. Both are called from `method314` (the per-tile scene drawer) at L1204/L1207 and L1225/L1231, so one tile's draw runs the ground mesh, then the overlay mesh, then the model sites from 4.1c-2b.

**Correction to the 4.1c-2b write-up, recorded rather than quietly fixed.** Those 21 model sites are in **`method314`** (decl L1142), not `method313` (decl L955 - the queue/culling method that CALLS `method314`), and there is one further caller: a delegating wrapper at `Animable.java:18`. ⚠️ **The plan's own reconnaissance already had this right** (`method313` per-tick render 186 lines vs `method314` ground/tiles 465 lines, at L331), so the error was mine and it contradicted the plan. **4.1c-2b's implementation is unaffected - the hook sits inside `Model.method443`, so it covers every route no matter which method calls it - but the description was wrong and that is worth stating.**

⚠⚠ **The gate must be extended FIRST, and it is not optional: the textured path IS reachable in normal play.** `client.java:3812` sets `Texture.lowMem = false` under high detail, which is exactly the condition that enables the `method378` branches in both drawers. The 4.1c-2a gate pins **only** the flat path (`method374`), so `method378` - used at **5 of the 11** ground sites - would currently be covered by nothing but the live screen.

⚠ **A design constraint that makes this genuinely different from 4.1c-2b: an early return at the top of `method316` would break tile picking.** Unlike `method443`, `method316` does more than draw - inside its per-triangle loop it also runs the hover/tile-marker picking (`method318`, setting `anInt470/471` and `hoverTileX/Y` at L1949-1957) and sets `Texture.aBoolean1462`. So the hook belongs **just before the rasteriser branch, after the picking**, not at the method top - otherwise a future GPU path would silently drop tile markers and hover.

**Shape:** one hook per drawer, placed before the rasteriser branch, dispatching the already-projected triangle (coords, colours, texture id) through a `SceneRasterizer` operation and covering all three variants (`374` flat, `378` textured, `374` lowmem-shaded) with one call each. ⚠️ **Do not dispatch the whole mesh at the method top**, for the picking reason above.

**AS BUILT (2026-10-05), and what this scope got wrong.** The call counts above were right (`method315` 7, `method316` 4), but the conclusion "**one hook per drawer**" was not: ⚠⚠ **the as-built shape is THREE hooks, because `method315` draws a QUAD as two separate triangles, each with its OWN picking block (`method318` x2) and its OWN 3-way branch.** So there are five picking/drawing decision points across the two methods, of which three carry rasteriser calls.

**The design constraint above turned out to be the whole basis of the change:** all three hooks were placed **AFTER** the picking, so a future rasteriser controls WHAT IS SUBMITTED without controlling whether a tile is hovered - the property the live gate then confirmed (markers and hover still work). ⚠️ **The neutrality proof is `git diff -w`: 19 insertions / 0 deletions against a raw 59/40, i.e. the 40 raw deletions are nothing but the re-indentation of the wrapped chain.** ⚠ **My first insertion attempt produced mangled indentation and was reverted rather than committed, which is why the status block quotes the whitespace-ignoring figure.**

**Coverage after this step, stated so it is not over-read:** the scene rasteriser is seamed for **SUBMISSION** - models via `Model.method443`, ground via `method315`/`method316` - which is what a GPU path needs. It is **not** a claim that every rasteriser entry point is seamed: `WorldController.method319`/`method321`/`method305-309`/`method323-324` and `Model`'s own triangle helpers (`method472`, `method479`, `method483`, `method485`, `method480-486`) are untouched.
